| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Donald_Trump_series)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Donald_Trump_series)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Donald_Trump_series) |

[Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s second and current tenure as the [president of the United States](24113.md) began upon [his inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Donald_Trump) as the [47th president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_presidents_of_the_United_States) on January 20, 2025. Trump, a [Republican](32070.md), previously served as the [45th president from 2017 to 2021](52231341.md). He lost re-election to [Democratic](5043544.md) nominee [Joe Biden](145422.md) in [2020](48410011.md), and then won against Democratic nominee [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) in [2024](59568546.md). Trump is the second [former U.S. president to return to office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_former_presidents_of_the_United_States_who_ran_for_office).[^cite_note-1] Alongside Trump's second presidency, the Republican Party also currently holds simple majorities in the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) and the [Senate](24909346.md), thereby attaining an overall federal [government trifecta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_trifecta#United_States).&#x20;

During 2025, Trump signed 225 [executive orders](210541.md), the most of any president in a single year since [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt). Many of these have been or are being challenged in court.[^cite_note-NBCNewYork2025-2] His [attempts to expand presidential power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_executive_theory#Growth_of_presidential_powers) and [conflict with the courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_affairs_of_the_second_Trump_presidency) have been described as a defining characteristic of his second presidency.[^cite_note-3] On [immigration](15051.md), Trump signed the [Laken Riley Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laken_Riley_Act) into law, [attempted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Order_14160) to restrict [birthright citizenship](7545952.md), and ordered [mass deportations](78314324.md) of immigrants. In January 2025, Trump launched the [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency) (DOGE), with [Elon Musk](909036.md) briefly overseeing it. DOGE was tasked with reducing federal spending and shrinking the size of the government, and it oversaw [mass layoffs of civil servants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_federal_mass_layoffs), as well as closing government agencies such as the [Agency for International Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agency_for_International_Development). Trump also signed the [One Big Beautiful Bill Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Big_Beautiful_Bill_Act) into law, which permanently extended [the tax cuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_Cuts_and_Jobs_Act) introduced in the first Trump administration, while increasing the deficit by around \$3 trillion by 2034.[^cite_note-4] Trump has also overseen [a series of tariff increases and pauses](78448911.md), which led to retaliatory tariffs from other countries and [stock market volatility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_stock_market_crash).[^cite_note-5][^cite_note-AP,_Trump_agrees_to_pause_tariffs_on_Canada_and_Mexico,_February_3,_2025-6][^cite_note-Financial_Post,_Canada_to_place_retaliatory_25_per_cent,_February_1,_2025-7][^cite_note-8]&#x20;

In international affairs, Trump has further pursued an expansionist and aggressive foreign policy, including the greater use of military force. His administration [increased support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md), [struck Houthi targets in Yemen](79465918.md) from March to May 2025 and aided [Israel](9282173.md) in the June 2025 [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War), during which he carried out [strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](80262844.md).[^cite_note-9] In early October 2025, Trump's [plan for a Gaza ceasefire deal](81215079.md) between Israel and [Hamas](13913.md) was signed. Trump has authorized a series of [strikes on suspected drug traffickers](80956668.md) in the [Caribbean Sea](7822.md), the legality of which is widely disputed under both U.S. and international law, and subsequently ordered [a military intervention](82021123.md) to overthrow and capture [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md), the [disputed](60825396.md) president of [Venezuela](32374.md), alleging that the Maduro government had links to narco-terrorist networks and conspired to flood the United States with cocaine.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11][^cite_note-12] In February 2026, he launched [a major attack on Iran](82533582.md) with Israel with the [stated goal of regime change](82756412.md), including [the assassination](82537558.md) of the [Supreme Leader of Iran](36814344.md) [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), which led to the [2026 Strait of Hormuz crisis](82550763.md) and [fuel crisis](82827398.md). As in his first presidency, Trump initiated the withdrawal of the U.S. from the [World Health Organization](33583.md), the [Paris Climate Accords](30242372.md), and [UNESCO](21786641.md).[^cite_note-13]&#x20;

The Trump administration has been criticized for [its targeting of political opponents and civil society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration). Many of his administration's actions have been found by the judiciary to be illegal and unconstitutional,[^cite_note-Savage_05232025-14][^cite_note-Sneed_01232025-15][^cite_note-Lucas_05022025-16][^cite_note-Jouvenal_07212025-17] and have been criticized as [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism_during_the_Trump_presidencies) and [fascistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_and_fascism). These actions have also been seen as contributing to [democratic backsliding in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States), as well as incalculable damage to the country's international standing. Trump, his family, and his administration have faced unprecedented conflicts of interest, self-enrichment, and [allegations of corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency). Trump is the first president with a [felony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felonies_in_the_United_States) conviction, having been [convicted in 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosecution_of_Donald_Trump_in_New_York) for [falsifying business records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falsifying_business_records) to conceal [hush money](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hush_money) payments. Having taken office at 78 years and seven months old, he is the [oldest person to become U.S. president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_presidents_of_the_United_States_by_age).&#x20;

## Milestones

### 2024 election

Main articles: [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign), [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md), and [2025 United States Electoral College vote count](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_Electoral_College_vote_count)

Further information: [2024 United States elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_elections), [2024 Republican Party presidential primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries), and [2024 Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_National_Convention)



2024 Electoral College vote results

Trump, who [previously served](52231341.md) as the 45th president of the United States from 2017 to 2021 and lost [his reelection bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2020_presidential_campaign) to [Joe Biden](145422.md) in the [2020 presidential election](48410011.md),[^cite_note-18] announced [his candidacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign) for the [nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries) of the [Republican Party](32070.md) in the [2024 presidential election](59568546.md) on November 15, 2022.[^cite_note-19][^cite_note-20] In March 2024, Trump secured the Republican nomination. [Trump selected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_Party_vice_presidential_candidate_selection) Senator [JD Vance](53396477.md) of Ohio, a former critic of his, as his [running mate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running_mate), and the two were officially nominated at the [2024 Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_National_Convention).[^cite_note-21]&#x20;

Early on November 6, 2024, the day after the election, Trump was projected to have secured the presidency.[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] Trump won the [presidential election](59568546.md) with 312 electoral votes and 49.8% of the popular vote, while [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) received 226 electoral votes and 48.3% of the popular vote.[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] Trump, upon taking office, became the second president in U.S. history to serve non-consecutive terms after Grover Cleveland in 1893,[^cite_note-26] and the first with a felony to serve the presidency after [his conviction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosecution_of_Donald_Trump_in_New_York) in May 2024.[^cite_note-WaPoFirsts-27] In the concurrent [congressional elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_elections), Republicans secured a [government trifecta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_trifecta) after retaining their majority in the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) and winning back control of the [Senate](24909346.md) for the first time since his first presidency.[^cite_note-28]&#x20;

### Transition period and inauguration

Main articles: [Second presidential transition of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidential_transition_of_Donald_Trump) and [Second inauguration of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Donald_Trump)



Incumbent president [Joe Biden](145422.md) and President-elect Trump in the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office) on November 13, 2024



[Chief Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice) [John Roberts](1928850.md) administers the [presidential oath of office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oath_of_office_of_the_president_of_the_United_States) to Trump in the [Capitol rotunda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Capitol_rotunda), January 20, 2025

The [presidential transition period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidential_transition_of_Donald_Trump) began following Trump's victory in the [2024 U.S. presidential election](59568546.md), though Trump had chosen [Linda McMahon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_McMahon) and [Howard Lutnick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Lutnick) to begin planning for the transition in August 2024. According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, Trump was "superstitious" and preferred to avoid discussing the presidential transition process until after Election Day. His transition team relied on the work of the [America First Policy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First_Policy_Institute), rather than [the Heritage Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Heritage_Foundation), a conservative think tank that garnered controversy during the election for [Project 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_2025), a set of initiatives that would reshape the federal government.[^cite_note-29] By October, he had not participated in the federal presidential transition process,[^cite_note-30] and he had not signed a required ethics pledge, as of November.[^cite_note-31]&#x20;

During the transition period, Trump announced nominations for his [cabinet](78310531.md) and [administration](44029.md). Trump was [inaugurated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Donald_Trump) on January 20, 2025.[^cite_note-32] He was sworn in by [Chief Justice](31739.md) [John Roberts](1928850.md).[^cite_note-33] The inauguration occurred indoors in the [Capitol Rotunda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Capitol_rotunda) as a result of a [severe cold wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_North_American_cold_wave).[^cite_note-34]&#x20;

### First 100 days

Main article: [First 100 days of the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_100_days_of_the_second_Trump_presidency)

In Trump's [first hundred days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_hundred_days_\(United_States\)) in office, he signed 143 [executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_order_\(United_States\)), the most of any president in this period, 42 [presidential memoranda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_memoranda), 42 [presidential proclamations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_proclamation), the [Laken Riley Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laken_Riley_Act), [a continuing appropriations act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_federal_budget), and other pieces of [legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation) for Congress. [Trump's extensive use of executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_orders_in_the_second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump) drew a mixed reception from both Republicans and Democrats. Some executive orders tested the limits of [executive authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_authority), and others faced immediate legal challenges.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36] Major topics Trump focused on included [immigration reform](78945778.md), [deportations](78314324.md), [applying tariffs on other countries](78448911.md), cutting federal spending, reducing the federal workforce, increasing executive authority, and implementing a [non-interventionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-interventionism) foreign policy.&#x20;

Trump's cabinet choices were described by news media as valuing personal loyalty over relevant experience,[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] and for having a range of conflicting ideologies and "eclectic personalities".[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] It was also described as the wealthiest administration in modern history, with over 13 billionaires chosen to take government posts.[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] He nominated or appointed 23 former [Fox News](11121.md) employees to his administration.[^cite_note-43] Notably, Trump's nomination of [Scott Bessent](38532044.md) as [Secretary of the Treasury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_the_Treasury) made Bessent the highest ranking openly LGBTQ person to serve in the United States government.[^cite_note-44]&#x20;

In March 2026, [The Wall Street Journal](173070.md) reported that Trump was planning to fire [Kristi Noem](27663933.md) after a Senate Judiciary Committee hearing during which she was criticised for her actions during her term of office, including her handling of the killings of [Renée Good](82055905.md) and [Alex Pretti](82203651.md), inappropriate relations with her advisor, [Corey Lewandowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corey_Lewandowski), and mismanagement of funds.[^cite_note-45] On March 5, Trump announced her reassignment to a new position, "[Special Envoy for The Shield of the Americas](82587150.md)",[^cite_note-46] and announced Oklahoma senator, [Markwayne Mullin](37205890.md) as her successor as the secretary of homeland security.[^cite_note-47] Noem is the first Cabinet official to be removed from her post during Trump's second presidency.[^cite_note-48]&#x20;

### Loyalty tests

Once the second Trump presidency began, White House screening teams fanned out to federal agencies to screen job applicants for their loyalty to the president's agenda. On his first day in office, Trump signed an executive order asserting to restore merit-based federal hiring practices and "dedication to our Constitution".[^cite_note-maga_loyalty-49][^cite_note-50] As part of its [U.S. federal deferred resignation program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._federal_deferred_resignation_program), the Trump administration demanded "loyalty" from federal workers.[^cite_note-51] In a break from politically neutral speech, the Justice Department issued memos about "insubordination", "abhorrent conduct" and vowed to pursue opponents of Trump's cost-cutting efforts "to the ends of the Earth" in what was described by current and former law enforcement officials as a campaign of intimidation against agents insufficiently loyal to Trump.[^cite_note-52]&#x20;

Staffers were dispatched across federal agencies to look for anti-Trump sentiment among government agencies. Some new hires were told to provide examples of what they did to help Trump's 2024 presidential campaign, when their moment of "MAGA revelation" occurred, prove their "enthusiasm", be positively referenced by confirmed loyalists, and provide access to their social media handles. *The Associated Press* described the intense loyalty tests as a way to separate individuals following traditional Republican orthodoxy from Trump's MAGA ideology.[^cite_note-maga_loyalty-49] Candidates for top national intelligence and law enforcement positions were given Trump loyalty tests. Candidates were asked to give yes or no responses to whether or not January 6 was an "inside job" and whether or not the 2020 election was "stolen". Those that did not say yes to both answers were not hired.[^cite_note-53]&#x20;

### Advisors

Trump had assistance from Elon Musk, other political operatives, and an antisemitism task force.[^cite_note-WSJed-54] Advisors were [Christopher Rufo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Rufo) in education; [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller_\(political_advisor\)) in domestic policy and immigration; and four co-authors of Project 2025: [Russell Vought](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Vought), [Peter Navarro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Navarro), [Paul S. Atkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_S._Atkins), and [Brendan Carr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brendan_Carr_\(lawyer\)).[^cite_note-55]&#x20;

## Executive orders

See also: [List of executive orders in the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_orders_in_the_second_Trump_presidency)



Trump signed the most first-day executive orders of recent presidents. First-day executive orders by previous ten presidents, 1969–2025

Trump began office with the most executive orders ever signed on the first day of a United States presidential term,[^cite_note-56] at 26 [executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_orders_in_the_second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump).[^cite_note-57] Trump's signing of executive orders was described as a "shock and awe" campaign that tested the limits of executive authority.[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] Four days into Trump's second term, analysis conducted by *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* found that nearly two-thirds of [his executive actions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_actions_by_Donald_Trump) "mirror or partially mirror" proposals from [Project 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_2025),[^cite_note-60] which was seconded with analysis from [Bloomberg Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_Government).[^cite_note-61]&#x20;

The signing of many of [Trump's executive orders are being challenged in court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_cases_related_to_Donald_Trump%27s_second_presidential_term), with the executive orders affecting federal funding, federal employee status, immigration, federal programs, [government data availability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_government_data_removals), and more. The majority of the early cases were filed in response to executive orders related to the establishment of the [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency) (DOGE), [Executive Order 14158](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Order_14158), and the actions taken by [Elon Musk](909036.md) and the DOGE team towards federal agencies described as cost-cutting measures.[^cite_note-62]&#x20;

Trump has signed executive orders requiring federal agencies to use specified terminology; these orders are only binding within the executive branch, yet have caused controversy. These renamings include "[Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md)" to [Gulf of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mexico_naming_controversy)", "[Lake Ontario](17947.md)" to "[Lake America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ontario_naming_dispute)", and "[artificial intelligence](1164.md)" to "super intelligence".[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64]&#x20;

## Economic issues, trade, and tariffs

See also: [Economic policy of the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration)



US unemployment rate since 2025



Change in non-farm employment rate since 2021

### GDP growth rate

In the first quarter of 2025, economic growth was a negative 0.5%. [CBS News](359551.md) said the decline occurred because "businesses scrambled to bring in foreign goods ahead of new U.S. tariffs".[^cite_note-CBS,_2nd_Quarter_GDP,_Aug_28,_2025-65] Second quarter economic growth was 3.8% annualized (as if continuing for the full year).[^cite_note-66] In the third quarter, [real GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_GDP) grew to 4.3%, the fastest rate in two years.[^cite_note-67]&#x20;

### Inflation

By May 2026, during the U.S.-Israeli war with Iran, inflation reached its highest level in three years according to government date, and consumer confidence reported by the [Consumer Sentiment Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Michigan_Consumer_Sentiment_Index) reached record lows.[^cite_note-68]&#x20;

### One Big Beautiful Bill Act

Main article: [One Big Beautiful Bill Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Big_Beautiful_Bill_Act)

The One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBBA) is a key part of the Trump administration's economic agenda. It permanently extends the individual tax rates [Trump signed into law in 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_Cuts_and_Jobs_Act), which were set to expire at the end of 2025. It raises the cap on the [state and local tax deduction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_and_local_tax_deduction) to \$40,000 for taxpayers making less than \$500,000, with the cap reverting to \$10,000 after five years. It includes several tax deductions for tips, overtime pay, auto loans, and creates [Trump accounts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_account), allowing parents to create tax-deferred accounts for the benefit of their children, all set to expire in 2028. It includes a permanent \$200 increase in the child tax credit, a 1% tax on [remittances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remittance), and a tax hike on investment income from college endowments. It phases out some clean energy tax credits that were included in the [Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_presidency)-era [Inflation Reduction Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_Reduction_Act), and promotes fossil fuels over renewable energy. It increases a tax credit for advanced semiconductor manufacturing and repeals a tax on [silencers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silencer_\(firearms\)).[^cite_note-Cooper_and_Dayen_2025-69]&#x20;

The OBBBA has been criticized for limiting health insurance coverage and resulting in an upwards transfer of wealth.[^cite_note-APNews2025b-70][^cite_note-71] It is expected to add a total of \$3 trillion to the national debt of the United States by 2034, according to the [Congressional Budget Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Budget_Office).[^cite_note-AP,_Budget_Act,_July_5,_2025-72][^cite_note-nbcoverview-73][^cite_note-APNews2025b-70][^cite_note-74]&#x20;

### Government shutdown in October–November 2025

See also: [2025 United States federal government shutdown](81225965.md)

Because the Senate could not muster 60 votes per [filibuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filibuster) rules, funding for non-essential services ended October 1, 2025. Throughout October and into November, Senate had 14 votes, all of which failed to reach the 60-vote threshold. The shutdown ended on November 12.[^cite_note-The_Hill,_Senate_rejects_measures_to_end_shutdown,_Oct_3,_2025-75][^cite_note-CBS_News,_How_the_longest,_Nov_13,_2025-76]&#x20;

In early October, [Senate majority leader John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune) said, "We have a majority of senators — 55 senators have already voted for this clean, short-term, nonpartisan CR," referring to a [continuing resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_resolution). [Minority Leader Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) spoke from the floor of the Senate and said 70% of Americans supported keeping the same money for the [Affordable Care Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_Care_Act), informally "Obamacare". In addition, Schumer claimed that a survey by [KFF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaiser_Family_Foundation) showed that 57% of [MAGA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MAGA) supporters also favored keeping the same money for Obamacare.[^cite_note-The_Hill,_Senate_rejects_measures_to_end_shutdown,_Oct_3,_2025-75]&#x20;

The governments of 25 states sued the Trump administration regarding SNAP benefits ("food stamps") which were expected to run out on the first day of November. 1 out of 8 Americans rely on these benefits. SNAP stands for "Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program".[^cite_note-Detroit_Free_Press,_Nov_6,_2025-77] The administration says that they are prohibited by law from using the "contingency fund" for normal, operational expenses because this money is purposed for extraordinary events such as [Hurricane Melissa](81393820.md). The states suing say this both goes against the wording of the law and is a "dramatic change", pointing out that the contingency fund was used for SNAP benefits during the 2019 shutdown.[^cite_note-78]&#x20;

On October 31, a federal judge in Rhode Island cited the [Administrative Procedure Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_Procedure_Act) and temporarily ordered the Trump administration to continue SNAP funding. A second federal judge in Boston said the Trump administration's plan to stop SNAP funding during the shutdown was against the law, but did not order payments to resume.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_asked_court_to_'clarify'_SNAP_ruling,_Oct_31-79]&#x20;

On November 7, [Justice Ketanji Brown Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Ketanji_Brown_Jackson) temporarily froze the lower court order requiring full payment of SNAP.[^cite_note-80] [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) stated, "at least nine states had already begun issuing SNAP benefits under the direction of the federal agency that operates SNAP," reportedly including California, Wisconsin, Kansas, Pennsylvania, New York, New Jersey, and Vermont.[^cite_note-81]&#x20;

In early November, Trump called for ending the Senate's rule and tradition of the filibuster.[^cite_note-82] On November 6, some Republican senators talked about making a "clean" Continuing Resolution one of the exceptions to the filibuster rule, but it's estimated that this change is unlikely.[^cite_note-83]&#x20;

On November 9, the Senate achieved a 60-vote compromise of a "mini-bus" which will fund certain departments through next September and the rest of government through January 30. SNAP would be funded through September 2026. Senate Republicans agreed to have a vote on the Obamacare funding by the 2nd week of December. Eight Democrats voted with Republicans to end the shutdown.[^cite_note-84] On November 12, the House passed the bill, 222 to 209. Trump then signed it into law.[^cite_note-85] [CBS News](359551.md) said, "The moderates viewed the deal — after Republicans leaders refused Schumer's offer — as the best possible offer they could secure, arguing that continuing the shutdown would only inflict more pain without any hopes of a better deal." In addition, [Senator Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine) (D-[Virginia](32432.md)) was won over by a new continuing resolution coming from the [White House](33057.md) which reversed the October layoffs.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_How_the_longest,_Nov_13,_2025-76]&#x20;

### Government funding starting January 30, 2026

Main article: [2026 United States federal government shutdowns](82240292.md)

On January 8, 2026, the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) voted 397 - 28 for a three-bill package, known as a "minibus", which House and Senate negotiators had put forward earlier that week.[^cite_note-CBS,_House_approves_partial_funding_package,_Jan_8,_2026-86]&#x20;

There was a partial shutdown from [January 31 till February 3, 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January%E2%80%93February_2026_United_States_federal_government_shutdown#Shutdown). On February 3, the House passed and President Trump signed the Senate version to fund the United States government through September, which the exception of the [Department of Homeland Security (DHS)](58236.md) only being funded through February 13. This is the result of disagreement between Democrats and Republicans over how to move forward from the [situation in Minneapolis, Minnesota](#Minneapolis).[^cite_note-NBC,_Trump_signs_bill_to_end_government_shutdown,_Feb_3,_2026-87]&#x20;

In the end, 21 House Democrats had voted for the funding bill and 21 House Republicans had voted against it.[^cite_note-88]&#x20;

### DHS partial shutdown starting February 14, 2026

A partial shutdown of the Department of Homeland Security began on Saturday, February 14, 2026. Most of the government employees affected will be expected to keep working, but their paychecks may be delayed depending on the length of the shutdown. This affects the Transportation Security Administration (TSA), Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA), Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA), U.S. Secret Service, U.S. Coast Guard, and Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE).[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Partial_government_shutdown_hits_DHS,_Feb_13,_2026-89]&#x20;

The disagreement between Republicans and Democrats in Congress pertains most recently to the [situation in Minneapolis, Minnesota](#Minneapolis) and more generally to immigration enforcement policies and actions by the Trump administration.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Partial_government_shutdown_hits_DHS,_Feb_13,_2026-89]&#x20;

Democrats are asking for judicial warrants before agents can enter private property, a ban on ICE agents wearing face masks, the mandatory use of body cams, and new laws for use-of-force standards.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Partial_government_shutdown_hits_DHS,_Feb_13,_2026-89] In fact, both President Trump and Homeland Security Secretary Kristi Noem have spoken in favor of body cams starting in Minneapolis and going nationwide as funding becomes available.[^cite_note-CBS,_officers_in_Minneapolis_will_start_wearing_body_cams,_Feb_2,_2026-90]&#x20;

### Trade and tariffs

Main article: [Tariffs in the second Trump administration](78448911.md)



Trump with [European Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Von_der_Leyen_Commission_II) president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) in Scotland on the day of the signing of the US-EU trade agreement, July 27, 2025

Trump has been a strong proponent of tariffs both during his campaign and as president.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] Throughout the first 100 days of his presidency, he implemented tariffs on multiple different countries, though mainly China, Mexico, and Canada, leading to retaliation.[^cite_note-93]&#x20;

On April 2, 2025, a day Trump nicknamed "[Liberation Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Day)", he announced a 10% universal import duty on all goods brought into the US (even higher for some trade partners), before ordering a 90-day pause shortly after drops in the market.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] The Trump administration promised it would achieve 90 trade deals during this period, though only managed to achieve two by the end of the deadline, July 9, as well as ongoing negotiations with China, extending the window until August 1.[^cite_note-96] Trump threatened to raise tariffs on multiple countries after the first deadline was met, including Japan and South Korea.[^cite_note-97]&#x20;



Upon imposing the highest U.S. tariffs since the Great Depression (called "Liberation Day" in April 2025), Trump claimed that "jobs and factories will come roaring back". However, manufacturing employment declined every month for the rest of the year.

[^cite_note-WashPost_20260115-98]



Though Trump claimed in December 2025 that "[inflation](38286.md) has stopped", the [consumer price index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index_in_the_United_States) (CPI) began increasing in the months following his April 2025 announcement of tariffs.

[^cite_note-CNN_CPI_20251211-99]



Consumer sentiment declined during the early part of Trump's second term.

[^cite_note-UMich_ConsumerSentiment-100]

&#x20;The University of Michigan Index of Consumer Sentiment reached a record low in April 2026.

[^cite_note-Axios_20260411-101]



Trump asserted tariffs on Chinese goods in February and April 2025, igniting a trade war that injected uncertainty as China turned to other sources.

[^cite_note-Grist_20260121-102]



In January 2026, the US dollar reached its lowest point in four years.

[^cite_note-FRED_202601-103]

&#x20;A lower dollar makes US goods less expensive abroad, but it also makes foreign products more expensive in the US and thus tends to increase inflation.

[^cite_note-Politico_20260127-104]



The price of oil was affected internationally at the outbreak of the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md).

[^cite_note-EIA_OilPriceData-105]

#### Immediate impact on market

&#x20;Tariffs were primarily absorbed by importers by compressing profit margins. Oxford Economics estimated that tariffs contributed about 0.4 percentage points to the 2025 September [Consumer Price Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index)'s annual rate of 3.0%, keeping inflation above the Federal Reserve's target. Corporate earnings were significantly affected, with global companies reporting more than \$35 billion in tariff-related costs ahead of the third-quarter earnings season.[^cite_note-106]



TSMC chairman and CEO [C. C. Wei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._C._Wei_\(business_executive\)) standing next to President Trump and [Howard Lutnick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Lutnick), announcing that TSMC is planning to invest in the U.S., March 3, 2025

In August 2026, financial markets reacted sharply to heightened geopolitical tensions after President Trump threatened to bomb Oman over the Strait of Hormuz, a move that coincided with the expiration of a 60-day ceasefire arrangement with Iran. On the first day of trading after these events, major U.S. stock indices posted broad declines: the Dow Jones Industrial Average fell 0.51 percent, the S\&P 500 dropped 0.52 percent, and the Nasdaq Composite lost 0.32 percent. Within the initial half-hour of trading, roughly \$200 billion in market value had been erased. At the same time, the yield on 30-year Treasury bonds climbed to 5.31 percent—its highest point since June 2007—as investors sought safer assets amid rising geopolitical uncertainty and persistent inflation concerns.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]&#x20;

#### *Learning Resources, Inc. v. Trump*

On August 29, 2025, the US Court of Appeals for the Federal Circuit ruled 7-4 that many of the Trump tariffs were invalid. The Appeals Court had ruled that the [International Emergency Economic Powers Act (1977)](2434972.md), did not grant the broad powers which the Trump administration was claiming. This decision did not affect some specific tariffs, such as steel or aluminum which were increased under other presidential authority.[^cite_note-BBC,_US_court_rules_many,_August_29,_2025-109]&#x20;

On February 20, 2026, the Supreme Court decided in *[Learning Resources, Inc. v. Trump](80234268.md)* that the IEEPA did not authorise the president to impose tariffs. All tariffs imposed under IEEPA were struck down.[^cite_note-cnn_decision-110][^cite_note-111]&#x20;

#### China

On May 12, the United States and China announced that tariffs would be reduced for a period of 90 days. U.S. tariffs on Chinese goods would be reduced from 145% to 30% and Chinese tariffs on U.S. goods would be reduced from 125% to 10%.[^cite_note-BBC,_Trump_says_US-China_relations_'reset,'_May_12,_2025-112][^cite_note-ABC_News,_Dow_closes_up_1,160_points,_May_12,_2025-113] However, this 30% is still more expensive for consumers in the United States compared to the state of affairs before Trump's initial tariffs.[^cite_note-NBC_News,_Dow_adds_1,100_points,_May_12,_2025-114]&#x20;

On August 11, 2025, this deal was extended for another 90 days.[^cite_note-CBS,_Trump_delays_China_tariff_hike_for_another_90_days,_August_11,_2025-115]&#x20;

#### EU

On July 27, 2025, the United States and the [European Union](9317.md) concluded a trade agreement, providing for 15% tariffs on European exports. The deal was announced by Trump and President of the European Commission [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) at [Turnberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Turnberry), Scotland.[^cite_note-BBCNews2025a-116][^cite_note-117] European states committed to \$750 billion in [energy purchases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_policy_of_the_United_States) and \$600 billion in additional investments in the United States.[^cite_note-118]&#x20;

#### Middle East

From May 13 to 16, 2025, Donald Trump undertook a [four-day visit to the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2025_visit_by_Donald_Trump_to_the_Middle_East), focused primarily on securing business deals and investments in the United States.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122]&#x20;

### Proposals for housing affordability

In January 2026, Trump proposed several actions and policies to make housing more affordable. These included the banning of "large institutional investors" from buying single-family homes, buying up to \$200 billion of mortgage debt in order to reduce mortgage interest rates \[already started earlier in the month], making 50-year mortgages available, making portable mortgages available, and allowing homebuyers to use their 401(k)s plans for down payments.[^cite_note-Business_Insider,_plans_to_make_housing_cheaper,_Jan_21,_2026-123]&#x20;

Critics say [the housing shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_crisis_in_the_United_States) is the number one reason for higher prices and that addressing this shortage will take a number of state and local actions. Critics also say that making portable mortgages available would give existing homeowners an even bigger advantage over first-time buyers.[^cite_note-Business_Insider,_plans_to_make_housing_cheaper,_Jan_21,_2026-123]&#x20;

In January 2026, Trump announced that he had ordered [Fannie Mae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fannie_Mae) and [Freddie Mac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddie_Mac) to buy up to \$200 billion in mortgage-backed bonds to drive down interest rates for new mortgages. The interest rates have since fallen.[^cite_note-Business_Insider,_plans_to_make_housing_cheaper,_Jan_21,_2026-123]&#x20;

## Domestic policy

Main article: [Domestic policy of the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration)

### Abortion

In January 2025, Trump reinstated the [Mexico City policy ("global gag rule")](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City_Policy), which had been rescinded by the [Biden administration](65732174.md). Since the 1980s, this rule has been put in place during Republican administrations and rescinded during Democratic administrations.[^cite_note-124]&#x20;

In May 2025, in a case involving [telehealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telehealth) prescriptions and the abortion-drug [Mifepristone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mifepristone), the Trump administration asked the court to dismiss the case, arguing that the states involve did not have [standing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_\(law\)). This case was *Missouri v. FDA* brought before [federal judge Matthew Kacsmaryk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Kacsmaryk) in the [Northern District of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_District_of_Texas). The states involved were Missouri, Idaho, and Kansas.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127]&#x20;

In June 2025, the [Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centers_for_Medicare_%26_Medicaid_Services) ended the rule which required hospitals to provide emergency abortions under the [Emergency Medical Treatment and Active Labor Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_Medical_Treatment_and_Active_Labor_Act).[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130]&#x20;

In October 2025, the [Food and Drug Administration](11632.md) approved a generic version of the abortion pill [mifepristone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mifepristone), allowing three U.S. companies to produce it and expanding access to medication abortion. The administration has not implemented major new restrictions on medication abortion, although [Health and Human Services](58250.md) officials announced a review of mifepristone's safety in response to pressure from opponents.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132]&#x20;

### Anti-LGBTQ+ policies

Main article: [Persecution of transgender people under the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_transgender_people_under_the_second_Trump_administration)

During his second presidency, Donald Trump launched a campaign of anti-LGBTQ+ and especially [anti-transgender policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_transgender_people_under_the_second_Trump_administration) that eliminated federal recognition of transgender people, stripped legal protections, and sought to erase trans identities from public life.[^cite_note-TheGuardian2025-133] Through [a series of executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_transgender_people_under_the_second_Trump_administration#Executive_Orders), the administration defined sex strictly by birth biology, banned trans people from the military,[^cite_note-134] restricted or defunded gender-affirming healthcare, censored research and education materials,[^cite_note-135] and targeted schools, universities, and cultural institutions accused of promoting "[gender ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_ideology)". Additional measures barred transgender athletes from sports, limited passport access,[^cite_note-136] and fueled international efforts to undermine trans rights. Accompanied by rhetoric portraying transgender people as a societal threat, these policies triggered widespread legal challenges, condemnation from human rights groups, and a surge in emigration and asylum claims by transgender Americans.[^cite_note-TheGuardian2025-133]&#x20;

### Environmental and energy policy



In May 2025, [NOAA/NCEI](37876.md) in the Trump administration indicated that it would no longer assemble the data that forms the basis of this chart.

[^cite_note-Guardian_20250508-137]

&#x20;NOAA/NCEI has access to non-public data, so that any private databases would be more limited in scope.

[^cite_note-Guardian_20250508-137]

Within hours of his January 2025 inauguration, Trump signed an executive order withdrawing the country from the 2015 [Paris Agreement](30242372.md), joining only Iran, Libya and Yemen as the only countries not party to the agreement.[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250120-138] The same day, Trump issued [Executive Order 14154](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Order_14154), "Unleashing American Energy", which included pausing funding for the [Inflation Reduction Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_Reduction_Act),[^cite_note-139] introducing uncertainty as to the [energy transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_transition).[^cite_note-140] His administration soon renewed a practice from his first term: removing mentions of climate change across numerous federal government websites that had been reinstated during Joe Biden's intervening term.[^cite_note-Guardian_20250204-141] In February 2025, the administration terminated the Environmental and Climate Justice Block Grant Program.[^cite_note-142] In June 2026, district judge [Richard Gergel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gergel) ruled that the terminations were unlawful and in July 2026 a federal judge ordered the program's remaining \$2.8 billion in funds be distributed.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] In April, Trump dismissed the scientists and experts who compile the [National Climate Assessments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Climate_Assessment) (NCAs) that are required by Congress, the next assessment having been planned for 2028.[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250428-145] The [globalchange.gov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Global_Change_Research_Program) website—established in 1990 to host legislatively mandated reports such as the NCAs—was taken down altogether at the end of June.[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250701-146]&#x20;

In May 2025, [NOAA](37876.md) said that its [National Centers for Environmental Information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Centers_for_Environmental_Information) would no longer update its Billion-Dollar Weather and Climate Disasters database beyond 2024, and that its information—going as far back as 1980—would be archived.[^cite_note-Guardian_20250508-137] In July, the chief administrator of the [Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) announced rescinding of the 2009 [endangerment finding](31102860.md#Endangerment), which concluded that planet-warming [greenhouse gases](21350772.md) pose a threat to public health.[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250725-147] (The endangerment finding is the [scientific determination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatology) that underpins the federal government's legal authority to [combat climate change](2119179.md).[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250725-147]) In concurrence with the attempt to rollback the endangerment finding, Trump's [Department of Energy](62866.md) released an assessment titled *[A Critical Review of Impacts of Greenhouse Gas Emissions on the U.S. Climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Critical_Review_of_Impacts_of_Greenhouse_Gas_Emissions_on_the_U.S._Climate)*. The report was assembled by five climate science contrarians and was heavily criticized for cherry-picked evidence, falsehoods, and distortions.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] The Union of Concerned Scientists called it "deeply flawed \[and] anti-science". Climate scientist Andrew Dessler called it "a mockery of science".[^cite_note-150] On February 12, 2026, the [EPA](58666.md) formally rescinded the [Endangerment Finding](31102860.md).[^cite_note-WashPost_20260212-151]&#x20;

In August 2025, the [Bureau of Land Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Land_Management) initiated a review of offshore wind energy regulations and revised its rules to favor fossil fuel production over renewables.[^cite_note-Mooney2025-152]&#x20;

On September 23, 2025, [Trump told the United Nations General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_speech_at_the_United_Nations) that climate change is "the greatest con job ever perpetrated on the world", that scientific [predictions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change) "were made by stupid people", and that renewable energy is a "scam".[^cite_note-TrumpUN_20250923-153]&#x20;

In October 2025, the Department of Energy cancelled 321 clean energy grants, totaling \$7.56 billion, issued in the Biden administration.[^cite_note-154] Trump administration officials stated in a July 2026 court filing that the cancellations were "based solely on the political identity" of the grant recipient's state.[^cite_note-155]&#x20;

In October 2025, the US used trade and visa threats to sabotage an agreement for cleaner international shipping at the [International Maritime Organization](14986.md).[^cite_note-Mooney2025-152]&#x20;

In December 2025, Russel Vought announced plans to dismantle the [National Center for Atmospheric Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Center_for_Atmospheric_Research).[^cite_note-Mooney2025-152]&#x20;

The Department of Energy issued several emergency orders in 2025 directing coal power plants to continue operating. The cost to ratepayers to maintain and run these aging plants was estimated at \$3 billion per year.[^cite_note-156]&#x20;

On January 7, 2026, Trump announced that the United States would be withdrawing from the 1992 [United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change](31898.md) (UNFCC), the UN's [Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change](15030.md) (IPCC), and 65 other international organizations—alleging the treaties "no longer serve American interests".[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260107-157] The next day, Trump's administration announced that the country would be withdrawing from the [Green Climate Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Climate_Fund), which since 2010 has provided funds to help poorer nations deal with the effects of climate change.[^cite_note-Politico_20260108-158]&#x20;

On January 14, 2026, the EPA revised its regulations on air pollution so as to no longer consider the dollar value of pollution's impact on human health when determining acceptable levels of polluted air for public health.[^cite_note-159]&#x20;

In June 2026, the Trump administration announced dismantlement of the [Ocean Observatories Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Observatories_Initiative) that includes 900 deep-sea instruments.[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260601-160] Since 2016, the project has been monitoring coastal environments, marine ecosystems and currents such as the [Atlantic Meridional Overturning Circulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Meridional_Overturning_Circulation).[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260601-160]&#x20;

#### Nuclear power

The Trump administration has sought to drastically expand the US's nuclear power generation, setting a goal to quadruple it by 2050. The administration seeks to revive production at decommissioned plants, such as the plant at [Three Mile Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Mile_Island_Nuclear_Generating_Station) in Pennsylvania. It also seeks to promote the construction of new [small modular reactors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_modular_reactor). The administration considers nuclear power generation a strategic priority because of its potential use in powering data centers needed to compete in the international AI development race. Experts are divided on the feasibility of Trump's nuclear power goals, with many expressing skepticism that they can be reached because of the high cost of nuclear power generation, while others say the conditions for financing nuclear power are relatively favorable. Despite efforts to increase nuclear power generation, data centers have led to steep increases in the cost of power in some communities, prompting local backlash.[^cite_note-161]&#x20;

### Domestic deployment of military forces

Main article: [2025 deployment of federal forces in the United States](80838047.md)

&#x20;The Trump administration has repeatedly deployed federal forces, including active-duty military personnel and federalised [National Guard](37988.md) forces, into certain U.S. cities. Trump has given multiple explanations for the deployments, saying they are part of crackdowns on protests, civil unrest, [crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_the_United_States), [homelessness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_United_States), and [illegal immigration](5044573.md). The actions targeted [Democratic Party](5043544.md)-led cities and sparked significant controversy, with critics labeling them as [abuses of power](4308572.md) and potential violations of laws like the [Posse Comitatus Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Posse_Comitatus_Act), which limits military involvement in domestic law enforcement.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] The moves came amidst broader expansions of the military's domestic use during the second Trump administration,[^cite_note-Broadwater_06112025-165] and Trump's prior comments during his presidential campaign to use the military to end civil unrest and protests without consent from state governors and target "[the enemy within](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_column)".[^cite_note-Savage_07082025-166][^cite_note-Pilkington_06092025-167]



Trump and FBI Director [Kash Patel](62202727.md) (far right) in a press conference on [crime in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_Washington,_D.C.), in August 2025

During the [June 2025 Los Angeles protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Los_Angeles_protests_against_mass_deportation) against local immigration raids, Trump federalized the California National Guard and deployed them to Los Angeles along with 700 [Marines](17349325.md) under [Joint Task Force 51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Task_Force_51).&#x20;

[Declaring a crime emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaring_a_Crime_Emergency_in_the_District_of_Columbia) in August 2025, the Trump administration deployed 2,000 National Guard soldiers to [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-168]&#x20;

In September 2025, Trump announced the deployment of 150 unarmed national guardsmen to [Memphis, Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis,_Tennessee), saying that the city "is deeply troubled".[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] Their presence in Memphis started on October 1.&#x20;

Louisiana governor [Jeff Landry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Landry) requested the deployment of the National Guard for large upcoming events: the [Bayou Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayou_Classic) college football event November 27–29, New Year Eve and Day celebrations, and Mardi Gras celebrations in February. The New Orleans police superintendent said, "We are working together and planning their deployment." New Orleans had been the site of the [January 1, 2025, terrorism attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_New_Orleans_truck_attack) in which 14 persons lost their lives and at least 57 persons were injured.[^cite_note-CBS,_expected_in_New_Orleans,_Oct_21-171]&#x20;

### FEMA

In April, the [Federal Emergency Management Agency](73797.md) denied an extension of benefits for areas in Georgia and North Carolina which had been hit by [Hurricane Helene](77945659.md) in September 2024.[^cite_note-Newsweek,_Trump_denies_FEMA_aid_extension,_April_12,_2025-172]&#x20;

Tornadoes hit parts the state of Mississippi in March and a major disaster declaration by the federal government took more than two months, even at the request of Republican governor [Tate Reeves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tate_Reeves) of Mississippi.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_While_Trump_overhauls_FEMA,_May_20,_2025-173] On May 23, the Trump administration approved disaster aid for areas within 8 states including Mississippi, as well as Nebraska, Iowa, Missouri, Kansas, [Arkansas](1930.md), Oklahoma, and Texas.[^cite_note-AP_News,_Trump_approves_FEMA_disaster_relief_for_8_states,_May_23,_2025-174]&#x20;

### Health policy

See also: [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention § Second Trump administration](6811.md#Second_Trump_administration)

Trump and his administration's [Make America Healthy Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Make_America_Healthy_Again) agenda promoted various [anti-science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-science) and [anti-vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-vaccine_activism) claims, which led to a resurgence of whooping cough and measles. They alleged they were working against [Big Pharma](560876.md).[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176]&#x20;

On November 14, Trump announced that he would nominate [Robert F. Kennedy Jr.](522298.md) for Health and Human Services Secretary. This was controversial given Kennedy's repeated endorsement of [anti-vaccine conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-vaccine_conspiracy_theories). The director of the [American Public Health Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Public_Health_Association), America's largest organization of public health professionals, said, "He is not competent by training, management skills, temperament or trust to have this job."[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-Reuters,_Trump_with_RFK,_Jr.,_December_2024-178]&#x20;

On February 18, Trump signed an executive order calling for the policy recommendations for reducing the out-of-pocket costs of [In Vitro Fertilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_vitro_fertilisation).[^cite_note-Superville2025-179][^cite_note-180] On February 25, Trump signed an executive order to improve healthcare cost transparency.[^cite_note-181]&#x20;



Trump with families of [overdose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_overdose) victims after signing the HALT Fentanyl Act, July 16, 2025

By late April, the Trump administration had placed on leave and then temporarily rehired federal employees in the NIOSH, or National Institute of Occupational Safety and Health, who had been involved in monitoring for black lung disease.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_'I_want_to_live':_Coal_miners_speak,_April_29,_2025-182]&#x20;

On June 9, Kennedy fired all 17 members of the [Advisory Committee for Immunization Practices](4211667.md). He claimed that it "has become little more than a rubber stamp for any vaccine". [Senator Bill Cassidy](9386382.md), a medical doctor, said "now the fear is that the ACIP will be filled up with people who know nothing about vaccines except suspicion." These firings came before a scheduled June 25 meeting in which the committee was expected to issue new recommendations for vaccines including COVID-19.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_RFK_Jr._removes_every_member_of_CDC_vaccine_advisory_committee,_June_9,_2025-183][^cite_note-BBC_News,_RFK_Jr_sacks_entire_US_vaccine_committee,_June_10,_2025-184]&#x20;

See also: [2025 United States government online resource removals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_government_online_resource_removals)

In late January, several CDC websites, pages, and datasets related to HIV and STI prevention, and LGBT and youth health [became unavailable for viewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_government_data_removals).[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186][^cite_note-Faust-187]&#x20;

In mid-February, around 1,300 CDC employees were laid off.[^cite_note-188] In April, it was reported that among the reductions were the elimination of the Freedom of Information Act team, the Division of Violence Prevention, labs involved in testing for antibiotic resistance, and the team responsible for recalls of hazardous infant products. Additional cuts affected the technology branch of the Center for Forecasting and Outbreak Analytics, which was established during the COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-sdfsadfg-189]&#x20;

On June 25, 2025, Kennedy announced that the U.S. was stopping its donations to the Gavi vaccine alliance, until Gavi can better demonstrate vaccine safety.[^cite_note-AP_News,_Kennedy_says_US_is_pulling_funding,_June_26,_2025-190][^cite_note-CBS,_RFK_Jr.'s_halt_to_U.S._funding,_June_26,_2025-191] The United States had been providing approximately 13 percent of Gavi's budget.[^cite_note-The_Guardian,_RFK_Jr_will_be_'personally_responsible',_June_26,_2025-192]&#x20;

On August 5, 2025, Secretary Kennedy announced that he was stopping 22 vaccine projects using mRNA technology, including Covid, RSV, and bird flu. A critic of this funding halt pointed out that mRNA vaccines have the potential for faster roll-out.[^cite_note-AP,_RFK_Jr._pulls_\$500_million,_August_5,_2025-193]&#x20;

On December 5, 2025, it was no longer systematically recommended for newborns of mothers having never gotten [Hepatitis B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis_B) to get vaccinated against this infection. The memos sparked indignation among most health experts.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196]&#x20;

On February 24, 2026, more than a dozen U.S. states led by Democratic attorneys general filed a federal lawsuit against the Trump administration and its Department of Health and Human Services, challenging changes to the childhood vaccination schedule that reduced the number of routinely recommended immunisations and shifted some to "shared clinical decision-making".[^cite_note-197]&#x20;

On August 10, 2026, Trump signed an executive order requiring the [vaccine for measles, mumps, and rubella](69149.md) to be split into three separate shots, and recommending that "to the maximum extent feasible, all childhood immunizations should be administered at separate medical visits".[^cite_note-guard-10aug2026-198]&#x20;

#### 2025 CDC leadership dispute

On May 14, 2025, Robert F. Kennedy Jr. stated that lawyer Matthew Buzzelli is acting CDC director, though the CDC web site did not list that name.[^cite_note-faust-199][^cite_note-faust2-200]&#x20;

[Susan Monarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Monarez) was confirmed as CDC head on July 31, 2025, but on August 27, she was fired. Monarez disputed the legality of the firing, as it had not been carried out by the president, and it had been falsely reported that she had resigned. The president later officially carried out the firing.[^cite_note-201] The firing was due to her refusing to rubber stamp what were expected to be unscientific recommendations from the senior staff vaccine experts, as well as refusing to fire them. The dispute began over demands from Kennedy and his top staff for changing the recommendation for COVID vaccine to persons with higher-risk conditions and senior citizens only. The official recommendation also affects insurance coverage and whether vaccines are available in pharmacies. Senator Bill Cassidy called for the next meeting of the [Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices](4211667.md) to be postponed. He said, "Serious allegations have been made about the meeting agenda, membership, and lack of scientific process being followed for the now announced September ACIP meeting. These decisions directly impact children's health and the meeting should not occur until significant oversight has been conducted."[^cite_note-ABC,_standoff_over_Monarez_firing,_August_28,_2025-202][^cite_note-203] The next day, the Trump administration announced the selection of Deputy Secretary of Health and Human Services [Jim O'Neill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_O%27Neill_\(investor\)) as a replacement.[^cite_note-204]&#x20;

Following news of Monarez's ouster, at least four other CDC senior officials announced their resignations:[^cite_note-cnbc-27aug2025-205][^cite_note-th-27aug2025-206][^cite_note-guard-28aug2025-2-207]&#x20;

- [Debra Houry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debra_Houry), Chief Medical Officer

- [Demetre Daskalakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demetre_Daskalakis), Director of the [National Center for Immunization and Respiratory Diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Center_for_Immunization_and_Respiratory_Diseases)

- Daniel Jernigan, Director of the [National Center for Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Center_for_Emerging_and_Zoonotic_Infectious_Diseases)

- [Jennifer Layden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Layden), Director of the Office of Public Health Data, Surveillance, and Technology, which contains the [National Center for Health Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Center_for_Health_Statistics)

Dozens of CDC employees walked out of headquarters and protested in support of Monarez and the departing officials.[^cite_note-208]&#x20;

#### Claims about autism

See also: [False or misleading statements by Donald Trump (second term) § Press conference about autism, Tylenol, and vaccines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_or_misleading_statements_by_Donald_Trump_\(second_term\)#Press_conference_about_autism,_Tylenol,_and_vaccines)

On September 22, 2025, Trump and other U.S. Department of Human Service officials delivered speeches issuing a major agenda for combating [autism](29113700.md). Warnings were for doctors not to recommend during pregnancy the pain- and fever-reducer [acetaminophen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acetaminophen), which is commonly used as an ingredient in [Tylenol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tylenol#Questions_about_safety_during_pregnancy).[^cite_note-sept222025-209][^cite_note-sept222025credibility-210] These warnings were issued in spite of the fact that medical experts have found no link between autism and this ingredient, with autism generally established to be a result of complex neurological factors.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-CBS,_Trump_and_RFK_Jr,_Sept_22,_2025-212] *[Scientific American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_American)* has reported that fever itself in the second \[2nd] trimester is a risk factor for autism, and therefore the claims made by the Trump administration are counter-productive.[^cite_note-Scientific_American,_RFK,_Jr.,_Is_Wrong_about_Cause,_April_17,_2025-213]&#x20;

Also on September 22, Trump also spoke in favor of and the [FDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FDA) approved the use of the chemotherapy drug [leucovorin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leucovorin) to also help alleviate the symptoms of autism.[^cite_note-sept222025-209] However, the justification for this approval was based on limited evidence.[^cite_note-sept222025credibility-210] A [CBS News](359551.md) contributor said, "Not all children with autism have this defect, so there's a test you can do to assess whether that's what's at play. For those kids, leucovorin has been shown to help, particularly with speech, getting kids to be more verbal than they were before."[^cite_note-CBS,_Trump_and_RFK_Jr,_Sept_22,_2025-212] The [National Institute of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Health) (NIH) was also granted \$50 million in funding for 13 projects to help transform autism research through the proposed Autism Data Science Initiative.[^cite_note-sept222025-209]&#x20;

Two-thirds of the increase in autism are estimated by an April 2025 *Scientific American* article to be due to better diagnosis and the desire of parents and schools to get started with early intervention. However, this same article estimates that one-third is due to an actual increase in autism from a variety of factors such as mothers in richer countries being older on average at childbirth, the ability to keep more premature children alive and healthy, and small-particle air pollution during the 3rd trimester which can cause an inflammatory response.[^cite_note-Scientific_American,_RFK,_Jr.,_Is_Wrong_about_Cause,_April_17,_2025-213]&#x20;

On October 9, 2025, Trump and U.S. secretary of health and human services [Robert Kennedy Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Kennedy_Jr.) alleged a link between autism and [circumcisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circumcisions).[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-rfjrcircumsicisionautism-215] Kennedy cited a 2015 [Danish](76972.md) study to justify this claim.[^cite_note-216] The validity of Kennedy's assertion about circumcisions being linked to autism has also been challenged by scientists and medical experts.[^cite_note-rfjrcircumsicisionautism-215][^cite_note-217]&#x20;

#### Prescription drug prices

In December 2025, President Trump announced a deal in which nine major pharmaceutical companies will in 2026 begin offering "Most Favored Nation" pricing to state Medicaid programs. In return, the companies will receive a reduction in tariffs for three years. This announcement brings the total to 14 out of the 17 largest pharmaceutical manufacturers agreeing to this deal, leaving AbbVie, Johnson & Johnson, and Regeneron as the major holdouts. In 2026, the Trump administration plans to start a website named TrumpRx which will not sell drugs directly, but will give patients information and links on pricing. Trump officials said the deal also included more than \$150 billion for new investment within the U.S. In addition, several of the companies will be donating pharmaceutical ingredients to the [Strategic Active Pharmaceutical Ingredients Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_Active_Pharmaceutical_Ingredients_Reserve).[^cite_note-CBS,_deals_on_prescription_pricing,_Dec_19,_2025-218][^cite_note-CNN,_deals_w/_pharmacy_companies,_Dec_19,_2025-219]&#x20;

Senior citizens on [Medicare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicare_\(United_States\)) will see lower prices in 2026 from the first negotiated prices going into effect from the [Inflation Reduction Act (IRA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_Reduction_Act) signed in 2022 by [President Biden](65732174.md). This includes [blood thinners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anticoagulant) such as Eliquis and Xarelto and [diabetes drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diabetes_medication) such as Jardiance and Januvia. However, a negative feature of the Inflation Adjustment Act is, that since it penalizes year-to-year increases in drug prices, drug companies have responded by setting higher starting prices. One study found that "launch prices" for new drugs became approximately 50% higher over the three-year period from 2022 to 2024 (inclusive), with an expert saying this practice is likely to continue until it receives a policy response.[^cite_note-NBC,_2022's_Inflation_Reduction_Act_interplay,_Dec_25,_2025-220]&#x20;

#### Subsidies for Affordable Care Act ("Obamacare")

On January 8, 2026, the [House](70645797.md#House_party_summary) voted 230–196 to extend the higher [Biden-era](145422.md#Infrastructure_and_climate) subsidies for another three years. Without this extension, it's estimated that monthly premiums will double for many persons who are signed up for the [Affordable Care Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_Care_Act). One House Republican said, "I am voting in favor of this discharge and of this legislation to send it to the Senate, so that the Senate will have the opportunity to put forth a reform package that can pass Congress and become law."[^cite_note-The_Guardian,_8_Jan_2026,_House_votes_to_extend_ACA_higher_subsidies-221][^cite_note-ABC,_17_House_Republicans_vote_with_Democrats_to_extend_Obamacare_subsidies,_Jan_8,_2026-222]&#x20;

### Immigration

Main articles: [Immigration policy of the second Trump administration](78945778.md) and [Deportation in the second Trump administration](78314324.md)

See also: [Mexico–United States border crisis § Second Trump administration (2025–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border_crisis#Second_Trump_administration_\(2025%E2%80%93present\)), [Mexico–United States border wall § Second Trump administration (2025–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border_wall#Second_Trump_administration_\(2025%E2%80%93present\)), and [Visa and deportation controversies in the second Trump administration § Detention of activists](79561481.md#Detention_of_activists)





*Left:* [Kristi Noem](27663933.md), the United States [secretary of homeland security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_homeland_security) from January 2025 to March 2026\
*Right:* [Markwayne Mullin](37205890.md), the United States secretary of homeland security since March 2026



During Donald Trump’s first year back in office, the monthly rate of ICE deportation flights more than doubled, and increased to 79 countries compared with 45 during Joe Biden's final year.

[^cite_note-Guardian_20260526-223]

&#x20;Destination countries included many outside the Western hemisphere, some of them to African and Asian countries that had reportedly never received such flights in recent history.

[^cite_note-Guardian_20260526-223]



The rate of ICE detentions accelerated during the second Trump administration.

[^cite_note-USAfacts_ICEdetainees-224]

President-elect Trump stated his intentions to revive the [immigration policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_policy_of_the_first_Trump_administration) from his first presidency, including a [travel ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_travel_ban) on refugees from [Iran](14653.md), [Iraq](7515928.md), [Libya](17633.md), [Somalia](27358.md), [Sudan](27421.md), [Syria](7515849.md), and [Yemen](350939.md). Other policies included [expulsion of asylum seekers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Title_42_expulsion) by asserting that they carry [infectious diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infectious_diseases), deputization of [police officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_officers) and soldiers to assist in mass [deportations](412990.md), and the establishment of sprawling [detention camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internment).[^cite_note-225] Trump said "there is no price tag" to carry out these deportations.[^cite_note-226] On November 10, 2024, Trump announced that [Tom Homan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Homan) would be "[border czar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_czar)".[^cite_note-Collins-2024-227]&#x20;

While border crossings reached record highs during the first half of the Biden presidency, they fell to lower levels near the end of his term, then dropped even further at the start of Trump's presidency.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]&#x20;

Shortly after he became president on January 20, 2025, the Trump administration ended services for the app of [CBP One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBP_One), reinstated the [national emergency](298608.md) at the [southern border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border), ordered the armed forces to draft plans for deployment,[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] and began the steps towards labeling Mexican drug cartels as terrorist organizations.[^cite_note-232] Trump increased deportation authorities for the [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md), the [Bureau of Alcohol, Tobacco, Firearms and Explosives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Alcohol,_Tobacco,_Firearms_and_Explosives), and the [Marshals Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Marshals_Service).[^cite_note-233]&#x20;

He gave ICE the power to deport immigrants who had come to the United States legally under [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration) programs,[^cite_note-234] and established daily deportation quotas to ICE offices.[^cite_note-235]&#x20;

Trump also signed an [executive order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Order_14160) attempting to end [birthright citizenship](7545952.md) for children of unauthorized immigrants as well as immigrants legally but temporarily present in the United States. At least nine lawsuits have been filed challenging the order on constitutional grounds, and as of February 2025, four federal judges have issued preliminary injunctions blocking its implementation and enforcement nationwide.[^cite_note-Lee2025-236][^cite_note-Raymond2025-237][^cite_note-238] On June 27, the Supreme Court limited the ability of individual District Judges to issue injunctions against executive actions, meaning to pause executive action. The Supreme Court did not rule on the merits of birthright citizenship. District Judges can still issue injunctions in limited circumstances, such as for persons directly involved in a class action lawsuit.[^cite_note-BBC,_Supreme_Court,_June_27,_2025-239]&#x20;

On January 22, 2025, Trump ended the policy from 2011 which prohibited immigration arrests in sensitive areas such as courthouses, schools, churches, and hospitals, or during funerals and weddings.[^cite_note-240] NPR reported that a "growing number" of Democrat and Republican officials in cities, states, police departments, school districts and other local governments stated they would not assist in migrant raids citing public safety, civil rights, and administrative capability concerns.[^cite_note-241] On January 29, Trump signed the [Laken Riley Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laken_Riley_Act) into law, the first legislation of his second term.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-GomezLicon2025-243] On the same day, he signed a presidential memorandum to begin expansion of the [Guantanamo Migrant Operations Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guantanamo_Migrant_Operations_Center) to house up to 30,000 migrants under detention, separate from the high security military prison at Guantanamo Bay.&#x20;

On February 6, [U.S. Border Patrol](567453.md) chief [Michael W. Banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Banks_\(law_enforcement_officer\)) claimed that illegal border crossings were already down almost 90% since Trump's inauguration, and that criminal prosecutions of those apprehended were up more than 50%.[^cite_note-FOX20250206-244] On February 25, Trump announced that the US would launch "[Gold Card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Gold_Card)" residency permits for wealthy immigrants for a price of US\$5 million, with an estimated release near the end of March 2025.[^cite_note-245] Trump deported 37,660 people during his first month in office.[^cite_note-246]&#x20;

In response to injunctions countering his deportations, Trump considered suspending [*habeas corpus*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_corpus_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-247] [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller_\(political_advisor\)) in May 2025 said regarding immigration cases that "*habeas corpus* can be suspended in a time of invasion", and that the Trump administration was "actively looking at" carrying out such a suspension, depending on "whether the courts do the right thing or not"; [Article One of the United States Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_One_of_the_United_States_Constitution) forbids such a suspension "unless when in cases of rebellion or invasion the public safety may require it".[^cite_note-248]&#x20;

On July 1, Trump toured a newly built facility in the Florida Everglades nicknamed "[Alligator Alcatraz](80275802.md)". This facility is designed to keep 3,000 persons in detention.[^cite_note-BBC,_Trump_visits-249] On July 10, the Department of Health and Human Services announced they would be restricting undocumented immigrants from enrolling for [Head Start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_Start_\(program\)), a federally funded U.S. preschool program.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

The administration announced on August 21, 2025, that it would be reviewing all 55 million visa holders in the United States.[^cite_note-251]&#x20;

In October 2025, the White House set a record-low refugee admissions cap of 7,500 for the 2026 fiscal year, primarily for white Afrikaners from South Africa.[^cite_note-Melimopoulos2026-252]&#x20;

By December 2025, the Department of Homeland Security reported the deportation of over 605,000 individuals, while an additional 1.9 million people voluntarily left the country. This resulted in a net negative immigration flow in 2025, marking the first such occurrence in 50 years.
