According to [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution), the net loss of immigrants ranged from 10,000 to 295,000 people.[^cite_note-Melimopoulos2026-252]&#x20;

In January 2026, the U.S. suspended immigrant visa processing for 75 countries, citing concerns over potential financial burdens and a need to reassess immigration procedures to prevent reliance on public welfare.[^cite_note-Melimopoulos2026-252]&#x20;

In February 2026, the Global Entry program that expedites U.S. Customs and Immigration clearance for pre-approved low-risk travelers was suspended to "preserve limited funds and personnel". However, the Trump administration reinstated it on March 11, 2026.[^cite_note-253]&#x20;

The immigration policies of the second Trump administration have drawn legal challenges from federal courts and Democratic-led states, particularly regarding visa and green card restrictions. The administration encountered two notable setbacks to its immigration agenda when a federal judge halted proposed visa caps and a coalition of Democratic-led states pursued legal action against broader residency restrictions.[^cite_note-a-254]&#x20;

On 14 September 2026, a federal judge blocked the administration from imposing fixed time limits on the length of stay for foreign students and journalists without an application for extension. In a separate case, a broad coalition of states filed suit to stop a federal regulation that would permit immigration authorities to deny green cards to immigrants who lawfully use public benefits.[^cite_note-a-254]&#x20;

These rulings came shortly before the Department of Homeland Security (DHS) regulation was set to take effect on 15 September. Under the proposed limitation, F visas for international students and J visas for cultural exchange programmes in the United States would have been capped at four years. I visas for journalists, which had previously remained valid for years, would have been limited to 240 days.[^cite_note-a-254]&#x20;

### Labor policy

#### Worker safety

A rule from 2024 had been scheduled to go into effect in Spring of 2025 lowering the allowable silica dust from 100 to 50 micrograms per cubic meter of air averaged over an 8-hour shift, with 50 being the standard already enforced by [OSHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSHA) in other industries. However, industry lawsuits delayed the rule in April 2025, and the Trump administration did not energetically defend the new standard. In addition, a group of seven Republicans in the [U.S. House](19468510.md) led by [Tim Walberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Walberg) ([MI-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan%27s_5th_congressional_district)) signed a letter to the [Mine Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine_Safety_and_Health_Administration) saying the new rule ignored cheaper solutions such as job rotation.[^cite_note-CBS,_coal_miners,_Nov_8,_2025-255]&#x20;

In 2018, [National Institute for Occupational Safety and Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_for_Occupational_Safety_and_Health) reported that approximately one in five coal miners with at least 25 years' experience in central [Appalachia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachia) had [black lung disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_lung_disease). However, among 11,000 workers who sought chest X-rays from 2020 to 2025, approximately 55% had some form of black lung.[^cite_note-CBS,_coal_miners,_Nov_8,_2025-255]&#x20;

In April 2026, the administration proposed eliminating the [Chemical Safety Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Chemical_Safety_Board), cutting the [Occupational Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_Safety_and_Health_Administration) budget by 7.5%, and cutting the [Mine Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine_Safety_and_Health_Administration) budget by 10%.[^cite_note-256]&#x20;

### Mass federal layoffs and firings

Main article: [2025 United States federal mass layoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_federal_mass_layoffs)

On February 13, Charles Ezell, acting director of the U.S. [Office of Personnel Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Office_of_Personnel_Management), signed a directive instructing federal agencies to dismiss probationary employees—generally, federal workers who have held their jobs less than a year, or had been promoted into the [excepted service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excepted_service) during that time.[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] Ezell told agencies to tell the fired employees that their performance was inadequate, and that they needed to cite no evidence.[^cite_note-259]&#x20;

As of May 12, 2025, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* tracked more than 58,000 confirmed cuts, more than 76,000 employee buyouts, and more than 149,000 other planned reductions; cuts total 12% of the 2.4 million civilian federal workers.[^cite_note-Shao/NYT-260] As of July 14, 2025, *[CNN](62028.md)* has tracked more than 128,000 workers laid off or targeted for layoffs.[^cite_note-CNN-tracker-261] The administration took back some layoffs such as for bird flu and nuclear safety.[^cite_note-Forbes,_Feb_26,_2025-262]&#x20;

In December 2025, the Office of Personnel Management launched the [United States Tech Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Tech_Force), a two-year hiring initiative intended to recruit about 1,000 technologists (including AI engineers) for federal technology modernization projects across multiple agencies.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264]&#x20;

Trump oversaw mass firings of federal workers at various agencies, many of them described as breaking with precedent or federal law and with the intent to replace them with workers more aligned with Trump's agenda.[^cite_note-Kinnard_1282025-265][^cite_note-Collinson_1282025-266][^cite_note-Savage_1282025-267][^cite_note-268] On January 24, 2025, less than a week into Trump's second presidency, he fired 17 independent [inspectors general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Inspector_General_\(United_States\)) at federal agencies, which appeared to violate federal law that requires advance notice of dismissals to both chambers of congress with reasons given 30 days in advance.[^cite_note-269] Trump also fired all Democratic but not Republican members of the [Privacy and Civil Liberties Oversight Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privacy_and_Civil_Liberties_Oversight_Board), which would prevent the board from meeting quorum and functioning.[^cite_note-Savage_1252025-270] Trump also fired members of the [Equal Employment Opportunity Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal_Employment_Opportunity_Commission), [National Labor Relations Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Board) and 56 senior officials at [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) for allegedly attempting to thwart his priorities.[^cite_note-Kinnard_1282025-265]&#x20;

### National security

#### Counterterrorism policies

##### National Security Presidential Memorandum-7

Main article: [NSPM-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSPM-7)



[National Security Presidential Memorandum–7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSPM-7) (NSPM-7), which designates the [anti-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-fascism) movement [antifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antifa_\(United_States\)) as a [domestic terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_terrorism_in_the_United_States), has been described as "Trump's new legal weapon for prosecuting protestors".

[^cite_note-NPR2026-271]

Trump issued [NSPM-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSPM-7) (National Security Presidential Memorandum-7) on September 25, 2025, directing a government-wide strategy to counter what it characterizes as organized domestic terrorism and political violence. The memorandum cites "common threads" such as "anti-Americanism", "anti-capitalism", "anti-Christianity", support for 'overthrowing' the United States Government, and extremism related to migration, race, and gender. It assigns the National Joint Terrorism Task Force to lead investigations, directs the [United States Department of Justice](52563.md) to prioritize prosecutions and consider domestic terrorist organization designations, and instructs the Department of the Treasury and the Internal Revenue Service to disrupt financial networks, while noting that the directive creates no enforceable rights. Thirty-one members of Congress later warned in a letter that NSPM-7 raises constitutional and civil-liberties concerns if used to target political dissent or ideological speech.[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275]&#x20;

#### Punishing states with cuts to Homeland Security funding

The U.S. Department of Homeland Security and the Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) reduced more than \$233 million from Connecticut, Delaware, Massachusetts, Minnesota, New York, Rhode Island, Vermont, and Washington state because they had failed to cooperate with Trump immigration policy. In addition, funding was cut to the District of Columbia.[^cite_note-ABC,_Judge_rules_you_can't_cut_security_funding_as_payback,_Dec_23,_2025-276]&#x20;

In December, a federal judge ruled that the Trump administration could not do this, stating in her decision, "To hold hostage funding for programs like these based solely on what appear to be defendants' political whims is unconscionable and, at least here, unlawful."[^cite_note-ABC,_Judge_rules_you_can't_cut_security_funding_as_payback,_Dec_23,_2025-276]&#x20;

### Social Security

On March 18, 2025, the Social Security Administration announced they would be implementing tighter identity requirements starting March 31,[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] with this date pushed forward to April 14. The new policy requires takes away the telephone option and requires individuals to either apply online or appear in-person at a field office.[^cite_note-Social_Security_press_release,_March_26,_2025-279] Applications for SSDI, Medicare, or SSI are exempted from this in-person requirement, along with applicants subject to extreme situations "such as terminal cases or prisoner pre-release scenarios".[^cite_note-Social_Security_press_release,_March_26,_2025-279] This new policy comes at a time the Trump administration is closing some field offices and laying off some Social Security staff.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Social_Security_says,_Mar_26,_2025-280]&#x20;

In late March, *Wired* reported that DOGE was putting together a team to migrate the Social Security base code from [COBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COBOL) to a more modern programming language, with the goal of achieving this in a matter of months, whereas most experts say it should take several years to do and test this safely.[^cite_note-Wired,_DOGE_Plans_to_Rebuild_SSA_Code_Base,_March_28,_2025-281][^cite_note-yahoo!_finance,_From_COBOL_To_Crisis,_April_4,_2025-282]&#x20;

It was reported in mid-April that the Trump administration had placed on the "Death Master File", renamed the "Ineligible Master File", more than 6,000 persons who are legal immigrants whom officials claim are either on a terrorism watch list or have an FBI criminal record. The White House, however, did not provide evidence for this claim.[^cite_note-AP,_What_we_know,_April_11,_2025-283]&#x20;

Social Security will "clawback" money from a disabled or retired person's monthly payments in cases in which overpayments are discovered. Overpayments can either be the fault of Social Security or of the recipient, for example, a person on SSDI disability not reporting monthly work income over a certain threshold. The Biden administration had capped the clawback rate at 10%, but this expired on March 27, 2025, reverting to 100%. On April 25, the Trump administration reduced this clawback rate to 50%.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Social_Security_to_reduce_overpayment_clawbacks,_May_5,_2025-284]&#x20;

### Universities

Main article: [Education policy of the second Trump administration § Actions against universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration#Actions_against_universities)

Further information: [Columbia University's settlement with the Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University%27s_settlement_with_the_Trump_administration) and [Harvard v. Department of Health and Human Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_v._Department_of_Health_and_Human_Services)



[Leo Terrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Terrell), the head of the Trump administration's [Task Force to Combat Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_United_States#Efforts_to_combat_antisemitism), with Donald Trump and Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) at the White House, April 7, 2025

In February 2025, [Leo Terrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Terrell), the chair of the Department of Justice's [Task Force to Combat Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Task_Force_to_Combat_Anti-Semitism), announced that he would investigate [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University), [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), [George Washington University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_University), [Johns Hopkins University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johns_Hopkins_University), [New York University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_University), [Northwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_University), [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley), [University of California, Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Los_Angeles), the [University of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Minnesota), and the [University of Southern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Southern_California) as part of the Department of Justice's broader investigation into [antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_and_antisemitism) on college campuses.[^cite_note-285]&#x20;

Trump's actions targeting higher education were described as part of an intimidation campaign against institutions viewed as hostile to his political views.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] He [targeted higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration#Actions_against_universities) by demanding it give federal oversight of curriculum and [targeted activists](79561481.md), legal immigrants, tourists, and students with visas who expressed criticism of his policies or engaged in pro-Palestinian advocacy.[^cite_note-288] Trump froze billions of dollars in federal funding for multiple universities in express defiance of existing laws prohibiting such actions without following proper legal processes that did not happen.[^cite_note-Economist_07302025-289] The deals and demands made by Trump were criticized as coercive, a shakedown, and legalized extortion in what *Axios* described as pursuit of a "cultural crackdown".[^cite_note-Basu_07302025-290][^cite_note-Economist_07302025-289] On September 3, Judge [Allison D. Burroughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allison_D._Burroughs) found Trump's efforts to freeze billions of dollars of funding for Harvard illegal, writing that the government had infringed upon Harvard's free speech rights and that it was "difficult to conclude anything other than that defendants used antisemitism as a smokescreen for a targeted, ideologically-motivated assault on this country's premier universities".[^cite_note-291]&#x20;

## Foreign policy

Main article: [Foreign policy of the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration)

Asked: Are there limits on your global power?

> &#x20; Yeah, there is one thing. My own morality. My own mind. It's the only thing that can stop me. I don't need international law. I'm not looking to hurt people.&#x20;

— Donald Trump, January 7, 2026

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260108-292]

&#x20;\
\
*New York Times* White House correspondents wrote that "Mr. Trump's assessment... was the most blunt acknowledgment yet of his worldview. At its core is the concept that national strength, rather than laws, treaties and conventions, should be the deciding factor as powers collide."

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260108-292]



A 2025 [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) study found that more than half in 19 of 24 countries surveyed, said they lack confidence in Trump's leadership of world affairs, with views about Trump differing sharply along ideological and partisan lines.

[^cite_note-Pew_20250611-293]



Among 24 surveyed countries, Trump's 2025 ratings trailed those of Joe Biden's 2024 ratings by an average of twelve percentage points in world affairs, though Trump fared better among right-wing populist parties in Europe.

[^cite_note-Pew_20250611p3-294]



A 2026 survey of European respondents showed net negative views of Trump concerning world affairs.

[^cite_note-Pew_20260623-295]



As of July 2026, [Trump has made 12 international trips](52890158.md) to 17 different countries during his second presidency.

Trump's second term foreign policy has been described as a mixture of both [imperialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperialism) and [expansionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expansionism) policies.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] He engaged in a [realist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realism_\(international_relations\)) and [isolationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolationist) "[America First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First_\(policy\))" foreign policy agenda.[^cite_note-Basu_2282025-300][^cite_note-Blaxland_2182025-301] His administration favored [hard power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_power) to achieve foreign policy goals,[^cite_note-Baker_02022025-302] and dismantled or withdrew support from domestic and international organizations dedicated to advancing American [soft power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_power).[^cite_note-Hvistendahl_07232025-303][^cite_note-304] The moves were described as ceding American global influence and creating a void filled by [Russia](25391.md) and [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-Hsu_06242025-305]&#x20;

His relations with allies were transactional and ranged from indifference to hostility, and he threatened them with economic tariffs or annexation.[^cite_note-Erlanger_2272025-306][^cite_note-Broadwater_2272025-307] He was described as taking the side of Russia in the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md),[^cite_note-Erlanger_2272025-306][^cite_note-Broadwater_2272025-307][^cite_note-308] and overseeing a rupture of the post-1945 rules-based [liberal international order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_international_order) and abandonment of [multilateralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilateralism).[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-Basu_2282025-300][^cite_note-Blaxland_2182025-301]&#x20;

[Michael Klare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Klare) wrote that [containing the influence of China](277880.md) and preventing the rise of any rival power is the central foreign policy objective of the administration.[^cite_note-310] Trump has attempted to deepen the [U.S.-India partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93United_States_relations).[^cite_note-311]&#x20;

Trump's administration saw large drops in global public opinion of the U.S.[^cite_note-Henley_06112025-312] Countries like Canada, Germany, the U.K., Denmark, and Finland warned their citizens about traveling to the U.S.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317]&#x20;

### Americas

Trump's second presidency has been characterized by a renewed American interference in the internal affairs of Latin American nations. These efforts have included both threats and promises made to influence the outcomes of elections in Honduras, Chile and Argentina, as well as tariff threats against Brazil and Mexico and military action against Venezuela.[^cite_note-318]&#x20;

#### Argentina



Trump with Argentina president [Javier Milei](56599840.md) on October 14, 2025

On October 20, the United States and Argentina agreed to a currency swap for up to \$20 billion. About President Javier Milei of Argentina and his upcoming October 26 mid-term elections, President Trump said, "If he loses, we are not going to be generous with Argentina."[^cite_note-Guardian,_\$20bn_currency_swap_deal,_Oct_20-319]&#x20;

The Trump administration also announced plans to increase beef imports from Argentina. The National Cattlemen's Beef Association objected to this plan with their leader saying, "If President Trump is truly an ally of America's cattle producers, we call on him to abandon this effort to manipulate markets." On October 22, Trump posted on social media that American ranchers need to help get beef prices down, and said that ranchers "don't understand that the only reason they are doing so well, for the first time in decades, is because I put Tariffs on cattle coming into the United States, including a 50% Tariff on Brazil".[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Cattlemen's_trade_group,_Oct_23-320]&#x20;

#### Boat strikes

See also: [United States strikes on alleged drug traffickers during Operation Southern Spear](80956668.md)

At Trump's direction, the United States has executed a series of [airstrikes](323167.md) in the Caribbean Sea on vessels alleged to be smuggling illegal drugs. As of January 4, 2026, a total of 35 strikes have been conducted, in which 115 individuals have been killed and 2 have been rescued after surviving the strikes.[^cite_note-321] Since the first strike, which was conducted on September 2, 2025, and killed eleven people, the Trump Administration has invoked combatting "terrorism" conducted by drug cartels as a justification for the strikes.[^cite_note-322]&#x20;

Many legal experts have argued that the strikes constitute illegal [extrajudicial killings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing) under both U.S. and [international law](8195726.md), noting that drug smuggling is generally considered to be a crime, not an act of war, and that the military is categorically prohibited from targeting civilians, even suspected criminals, unless facing a threat of imminent harm.[^cite_note-323] [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) lawyer and commentator [Andrew C. McCarthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_C._McCarthy), writing in [National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review), called the strikes "lawless" and "not legitimate under the law".[^cite_note-324] The [U.S. Department of Justice](52563.md)'s [Office of Legal Counsel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Legal_Counsel) defended the strikes in a memo that has not been released to the public, endorsing President Trump's claim that the United States is an armed conflict with Latin American drug cartels and arguing that the destruction of drug boats eliminates a source of revenue that could be used to purchase weapons for attacks against the United States.[^cite_note-325]&#x20;

On November 27, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* published an article alleging that Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) had ordered the officers responsible for the first airstrike on September 2, 2025, to "kill everyone" on board, prompting Admiral [Frank M. Bradley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_M._Bradley) to order a second strike to kill the two survivors of the initial strike, who were clinging to the wreckage of the boat that had been by the initial strike.[^cite_note-326] As [no quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_quarter) orders are forbidden under the [Law of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_war), the second September 2 strike was widely criticized as constituting a [war crime](48626.md) even under the Trump Administration's justification for the boat strike campaign in general, with the chair of the [United States Senate Committee on Armed Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Armed_Services), [Republican](32070.md) Senator [Roger Wicker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Wicker), along with ranking member [Jack Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Reed_\(Rhode_Island_politician\)), promising "vigorous oversight to determine the facts related to these circumstances".[^cite_note-327] According to [The New York Times](30680.md), five U.S. officials stated that while Hegseth did, on September 2, order the killing of all individuals aboard the alleged drug boat, he did not specifically address what was to be done if any individuals survived the initial strike and was not present when Bradley carried out the second strike.[^cite_note-328] After Hegseth refused to release a video of the second strike to [Congress](31756.md), the [United States Senate](24909346.md) placed a provision in the 2026 [National Defense Authorization Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Defense_Authorization_Act) holding back a quarter of Hegseth's travel budget until he showed the video to lawmakers.[^cite_note-329]&#x20;

#### Venezuela

See also: [2026 United States intervention in Venezuela](82021123.md)



US president Donald Trump with members of his cabinet at [Mar-a-Lago](1452754.md) during Operation Absolute Resolve

On January 3, 2026, the United States launched [airstrikes](82021123.md) in Venezuela, capturing Venezuelan president [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) and his wife,[^cite_note-330] and resulting in an estimated 80 casualties, including Maduro's personal guard, military personnel, and civilians.[^cite_note-331] The airstrike was done without congressional awareness or authorization, resulting in criticism from media and a number of congressmen.[^cite_note-332] Maduro and his wife were arraigned in a Manhattan federal court on 5, January 2026. Both pleaded not guilty to a number of drug trafficking charges.[^cite_note-333] Followed by a purge of Maduro-era loyalists from the Venezuelan government, which was taken over by [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md).[^cite_note-334] Since 2026, Venezuela has been a *de facto* [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) of the United States. The Trump administration, primarily through [U.S. Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_State) [Marco Rubio](5502549.md), maintains control over its domestic finances, foreign policy, government appointments, revenues, and oil reserves.[^cite_note-NYT_07112026-335][^cite_note-Callaghan_01042026-336]&#x20;

### Europe



Trump has emphasized good relations with Finland, especially with the "[icebreaker](483060.md) trade" in October 2025.

[^cite_note-337]

[^cite_note-338]

&#x20;He met with Finnish president [Alexander Stubb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Stubb) at the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office).

#### NATO spending

The Trump administration has argued that European nations should contribute more to their own defense while the U.S. focuses on China.[^cite_note-TheWashingtonPost2024-339] During his 2024 campaign, he said he would not defend [NATO](21133.md) allies if they did not meet the alliance's spending target of 2% of [GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP) on defense, and that he would "encourage" Russia to "do whatever the hell they want".[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] Trump officials privately expressed contempt for European "freeloading";.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-Smialek/NYT-344] Trump's policies and rhetoric accelerated an [ongoing European rearmament](79277430.md).[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] Trump called Poland, which exceeds the NATO defense spending target, "one of the best groups of people I have ever met".[^cite_note-349]&#x20;

#### Vice President JD Vance

Trump's administration has also expressed cultural disagreement with Europe and the [European Union](9317.md): in [a speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_JD_Vance_speech_at_the_Munich_Security_Conference) at the [61st Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/61st_Munich_Security_Conference), Vice President Vance criticized European policies on free speech and democratic values, and accused European leaders of suppressing dissenting views on issues such as immigration.[^cite_note-TheWashingtonPost2024-339][^cite_note-350] Vance also expressed support for lifting restrictions on the [Alternative for Germany](38708229.md), a far-right German political party.[^cite_note-351]&#x20;

#### United Kingdom



Trump with British prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) at [Turnberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turnberry,_South_Ayrshire), Scotland, July 28, 2025

While meeting with British prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) in February 2025, Trump accepted a request from [King Charles III](125248.md) for a [state visit](327283.md) in the U.K.;[^cite_note-BBCNews2025b-352][^cite_note-353] both Trump and Starmer praised the U.S.–U.K. "[Special Relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Relationship)".[^cite_note-BBCNews2025b-352] The state visit [occurred between September 16–18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_state_visit_by_Donald_Trump_to_the_United_Kingdom), 2025.[^cite_note-BBC4Sep2025-354] Relations became strained in March 2026 due to Starmer's refusal to join the U.S.–Israeli strikes on Iran during the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) or support the [U.S. naval blockade of Iran](82937256.md), prompting Trump to repeatedly condemn the prime minister.[^cite_note-355] King Charles III undertook a [state visit to the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_the_United_States) between April 27–30, 2026 and addressed Congress, aimed partly at alleviating these tensions.[^cite_note-356]&#x20;

#### Ukraine

Further information: [Peace negotiations in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present) § Developments under the second Trump presidency (since 2025)](70255183.md#Developments_under_the_second_Trump_presidency_\(since_2025\))

Trump began a push for [peace negotiations](70255183.md) to end the [Russia–Ukraine war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-357] Trump's first foreign visit as [president-elect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President-elect_of_the_United_States) was to Paris for [the reopening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reopening_of_Notre-Dame_de_Paris) of the [Notre-Dame de Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris), during which he met with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) to discuss the war,[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] and met with other European officials.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] In February 2025, [Trump held phone calls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_Putin%E2%80%93Trump_phone_call) with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) and with Zelenskyy that he said marked the beginning of negotiations.[^cite_note-guardian-20250212b2-362] He threatened Ukraine with a suspension of [U.S. military aid](70376708.md)[^cite_note-363] and Russia with sanctions and tariffs if he decided they were not negotiating in good faith.[^cite_note-MoscowTimesJan2025-364][^cite_note-365] The U.S. sought a [mineral resources agreement with Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93United_States_Mineral_Resources_Agreement)[^cite_note-NBC_minerals2-366] though was unwilling to offer postwar security guarantees for Ukraine in exchange.[^cite_note-NBC_minerals2-366][^cite_note-367] Negotiators reached a deal on the agreement[^cite_note-Tarasova-Markina_20250226-368][^cite_note-CNN_fulltext_20250225-369][^cite_note-Butenko_20250226-370] but the agreement fell through after [a contentious meeting between Trump and Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting) in the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office) that ended when the Ukrainian delegation was abruptly asked to leave.[^cite_note-BBCNews2025b-352][^cite_note-Bennett2025-371] Afterwards, the U.K. and France developed a proposal in which a "[coalition of the willing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_willing_\(Russo-Ukrainian_War\))" would provide security guarantees to Ukraine.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-Deutsche_Welle_20250302-373] The [U.S. and Russia held a summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_United_States%E2%80%93Russia_summit_in_Saudi_Arabia) in Saudi Arabia for peace talks in February[^cite_note-374] and U.S. held a summit with Ukraine the next month, during which Ukraine accepted a U.S.-proposed 30-day ceasefire.[^cite_note-Ravid2025-375][^cite_note-376] Putin did not accept the ceasefire,[^cite_note-377] though on March 18 Russia agreed to a 30-day ceasefire only for strikes on energy infrastructure and in the [Black Sea](3386.md),[^cite_note-378] which Ukraine agreed to.[^cite_note-InstitutefortheStudyofWar2025-379][^cite_note-BBCNewsРусскаяслужба2025-380]&#x20;



Trump and Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) at [NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit) in [The Hague](30269.md), June 25, 2025

On July 9, Trump reversed a previously suspended military aid package to Ukraine following Russia's largest aerial assault on the country, involving hundreds of drones and missiles. The suspension, reportedly authorized by senior defense officials without Trump's prior knowledge. Trump expressed growing frustration with Russian president Vladimir Putin, accusing him of insincerity and relentless aggression, saying, "He wants to go all the way, just keep killing people, it's no good." The U.S. are discussing sending 10 [Patriot missiles](52024.md) to Ukraine, and Trump said he is reviewing a [proposed sanctions bill](79879253.md) by Senator [Lindsey Graham](363279.md) that would impose 500% [tariffs](78448911.md) on countries trading with Russia. Although Trump has previously mentioned [secondary sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sanctions), none have been implemented so far, with the president citing their cost and waiting to see if a peace deal would emerge.[^cite_note-381] On July 14, 2025, Trump threatened to impose 100% tariffs and secondary sanctions on [countries purchasing Russian oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Russian_oil_products_sanctions_and_price_cap) if Russia did not agree to a ceasefire within 50 days.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] Trump confirmed a [summit meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_Summit) between him and Putin to be held on August 15, 2025, in [Alaska](624.md)[^cite_note-384] The high-level summit at [Joint Base Elmendorf–Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Base_Elmendorf%E2%80%93Richardson) in [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska) concluded without progress toward a ceasefire. Following the talks, Trump suggested the United States could assist Ukraine with surveillance flights or air defense support, while ruling out the deployment of ground troops.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]&#x20;



[EU, NATO members and Zelenskyy in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_White_House_multilateral_meeting_on_Ukraine), August 18, 2025

Trump emphasized that European nations should shoulder the primary burden of assistance and pressed Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelenskyy to take greater responsibility in securing peace. He further hinted that Ukraine may eventually face difficult choices, including potential territorial concessions, to bring the conflict to an end.[^cite_note-387]&#x20;

In September 2025, Trump urged Europe to stop buying [Russian oil](24648652.md) and start putting [economic pressure on China](2016720.md) for funding Russia's war effort.[^cite_note-388] Treasury Secretary [Scott Bessent](38532044.md) said the Trump administration is "prepared to increase pressure on Russia, but we need our European partners to follow us".[^cite_note-389]&#x20;

In September 2025, the Trump administration approved the delivery of the first two packages of weapons to Ukraine from US stockpiles totalling approximately \$1 billion, which would be paid for by NATO allies, under the new mechanism called [Prioritized Ukraine Requirements List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prioritized_Ukraine_Requirements_List) (PURL). The mechanism aims to deliver aid worth up to \$10 billion.[^cite_note-390] On September 27, 2025, President Zelenskyy announced a \$90 billion arms agreement with the United States in works.[^cite_note-391]&#x20;

On October 22, 2025, the United States imposed sanctions on Russian energy companies [Rosneft](1313566.md) and [Lukoil](1027881.md),[^cite_note-392] affecting their customers in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_and_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-393]&#x20;

In November 2025, Trump proposed a [peace plan](70255183.md#US_28-point_plan) that would require Ukraine to cede territory in the [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas), including Ukrainian-controlled parts of [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md). The plan would also freeze the front lines in [Kherson Oblast](1273370.md) and [Zaporizhzhia Oblast](494936.md).[^cite_note-394]&#x20;

### Africa

#### South Africa



Trump with South African president [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) in May 2025

Trump's administration strained [relations with South Africa](15996750.md). Trump suspended all aid to South Africa, saying the country's [land expropriation law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expropriation_Act,_2024) discriminated against [Afrikaners](18947965.md);[^cite_note-TheWhiteHouse2025-395] he also offered Afrikaners refugee status in the U.S.[^cite_note-TheWashingtonPost2024-339][^cite_note-guard-12may2025-396] Rubio refused to attend the [G20 foreign ministers' meeting in Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_G20_Johannesburg_Summit)[^cite_note-AlJazeeraEnglish2025-397] and he declared the South African ambassador *[persona non grata](521241.md)* for his criticism of U.S. policies.[^cite_note-Politico2025-398]&#x20;

#### Democratic Republic of the Congo and Rwanda

Rubio condemned the [Rwandan](25645.md)-backed [Goma offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Goma_offensive) in the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md) and called for an immediate ceasefire and respect for territorial integrity.[^cite_note-399] In April, U.S. officials entered into talks with the DRC on a minerals deal after a proposal from Congolese president [Félix Tshisekedi](57511508.md).[^cite_note-400]&#x20;



Signing ceremony for the peace agreement, Washington, D.C., June 27, 2025

In June 2025, President Trump brokered a [peace deal between the Democratic Republic of Congo and Rwanda](80332368.md), with the U.S. getting "a lot of mineral rights". This potentially brings to an end a 30+ year conflict dating back to the [1994 Rwanda genocide](676659.md). Each country had accused the other of financing and supporting rebel groups. On June 27, in a deal signed in the White House, each country pledged to end such support, allow the return of refugees, and create a joint security coordination mechanism aimed at resolving disputes.[^cite_note-ABC,_Trump_heralds_US-brokered_peace_deal,_June_27,_2025-401]&#x20;

An estimated 6 million persons have died in this conflict, and Trump said that this deal ends "one of the worst wars anyone's ever seen". A critic said, "It risks reducing peace to a transactional exchange. Minerals are only one driver of conflict." Secretary of State Marco Rubio said, "This is an important moment after 30 years of war. President Trump is a president of peace. He really does want peace. He prioritizes it above all else."[^cite_note-ABC,_Trump_heralds_US-brokered_peace_deal,_June_27,_2025-401] A former prime minister of the DR Congo, Joseph Kabila, expressed skepticism of the peace deal, saying that it was "nothing more than a trade agreement".[^cite_note-BBC,_DR_Congo-Rwanda_peace_deal,_June_28,_2025-402]&#x20;

#### South Sudan

In April 2025, the U.S. revoked all visas of and barred entry to citizens of [South Sudan](32350676.md) after a dispute over the deportation of a South Sudanese citizen.[^cite_note-Guardian_Apr_6,_20252-403]&#x20;

### Asia

#### Middle East

Further information: [2025–2026 Iran–United States negotiations](79685207.md), [March–May 2025 United States attacks in Yemen](79465918.md), and [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War)



Trump with [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan), [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md), and other Muslim leaders at a multilateral meeting at the United Nations, September 23, 2025

##### Houthis

In March 2025, the [U.S. began a series of airstrikes](79465918.md) on [Houthi](17240332.md) targets in Yemen to counter [attacks on Red Sea shipping](75207766.md), with the goal of restoring freedom of navigation and deterring further aggression.[^cite_note-404] U.S. officials accidentally included *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)* editor [Jeffrey Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Goldberg) in a [Signal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signal_\(software\)) group chat discussing the military plans, [sparking a political scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_government_group_chat_leak) and accusations of risking national security and violating records-preservation laws.[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407]&#x20;

In May 2025, Trump announced that his administration had reached a [ceasefire](592591.md) deal with the Houthis.[^cite_note-408]&#x20;

##### Iran

See also: [Iran–United States relations during the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93United_States_relations_during_the_second_Trump_administration) and [2026 Iran war](82533582.md)



Trump and Vance in the [Situation Room](925990.md) during [strikes on Iran](80262844.md)



The retail price of gasoline in the US increased at the outbreak of war in Iran.

[^cite_note-EIAgasPrices-409]

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260420-410]

&#x20;In late April 2026, Trump said Americans should expect higher gas prices for "a little while".

[^cite_note-411]



The retail price of diesel fuel in the US increased at the outbreak of war in Ukraine and Iran.

[^cite_note-EIA_diesel-412]

Trump reinstated his ["maximum pressure" campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximum_pressure_campaign) against Iran and sought [a new set of negotiations](79685207.md) to limit [Iran's nuclear program](721807.md).[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] In March 2025, Trump sent a letter to Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) urging new negotiations and warning of military action if talks fail.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] On June 13, 2025, [Israeli planes bombed sites in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) associated with Iran's nuclear program. President Trump stated he was not involved, although Iranian leaders have said they do not believe this. There have been reports that Trump vetoed a plan to kill Iran's Ayatollah Khamenei, and that the United States communicated to Israel that Iran has not killed an American and discussions of killing political leaders should not be on the table.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Israel-Iran_live_updates,_June_15,_2025-418]&#x20;

On June 21, 2025, the U.S. [attacked three nuclear sites](80262844.md): the [Fordow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fordow_Fuel_Enrichment_Plant) uranium enrichment facility, the [Natanz](43648558.md) nuclear facility, and [Isfahan](223149.md) nuclear technology center.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420] B-2 airplanes took off from the state of Missouri and made the 18-hour flight to Iran, being refueled multiple times en route. Entering Iranian airspace, fighter jets cleared space ahead of them, and a Navy sub fired more than 24 Tomahawk missiles. The leading B-2 dropped two Massive Ordnance Penetrators bombs (MOPs, or "bunker-busters") at the Fordo site. A total of 14 MOPs were dropped at two target sites. [Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) said, "We devastated the Iranian nuclear program, but it's worth noting that the operation did not target Iranian troops or the Iranian people."[^cite_note-421] On June 22, Trump said that since the Islamic regime in control of Iran has failed to make Iran great, it should be replaced to "Make Iran Great Again".[^cite_note-Guzman2025-422][^cite_note-423] Later, he posted on social media that their constant anger, hostility, and despair have only led them to ruin. The path they're on offers no hope, only more hardship, "I wish the leadership of Iran would realize that you often get more with honey than you do with vinegar".[^cite_note-424]&#x20;



Rescuers and residents at the site of the Shajareh Tayyebeh girls' school in [Minab](2392946.md), Iran, which was destroyed by a [U.S. military strike](82536519.md) on February 28, 2026

During the [2025–2026 Iranian protests](81976180.md), Trump repeatedly warned the Iranian authorities that the U.S. would "intervene" if the regime did not halt its [crackdown on protesters](82036668.md).[^cite_note-425] On January 16, 2026, Trump announced that the Iranian leadership had reportedly canceled over 800 planned executions.[^cite_note-426] On February 28, 2026, Trump launched a [major attack on Iran](82533582.md) with Israel with the [stated goal of regime change](82756412.md).[^cite_note-427] On March 21, 2026, Trump announced a 48-hour ultimatum on [Truth Social](69077351.md) calling for Iran to open the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md), threatening the destruction of Iranian power infrastructure.[^cite_note-428] Over the following week, specifically on March 23 and 26, Trump announced two different extensions to the ultimatum. On March 26, an official pause on "the period of Energy Plant destruction" until April 2, 2026, was announced on social media,[^cite_note-429] an extension he later stated was a result of Iran allowing oil-carrying ships to safely pass through the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-ABCNews2026-430]&#x20;

On April 4 and 5, 2026, Trump resumed posting threats of US attack on Iranian infrastructure, stating April 7, 2026, as the deadline for the Strait of Hormuz to be opened.[^cite_note-ABCNews2026-430] On April 7, Trump posted to Truth Social that "a whole civilization will die tonight, never to be brought back again",[^cite_note-431] causing domestic concern that Trump would use nuclear weapons, and resulting in a number of bipartisan calls for the 25th Amendment to be invoked.[^cite_note-432] The White House confirmed in an official statement that there is currently no consideration of usage of nuclear weaponry.[^cite_note-433] Following the failure of the [Islamabad Talks](82923583.md) to end the war, Trump imposed a [naval blockade on Iran](82937256.md) on April 13.[^cite_note-434] In June 2026, after concluding that continued hostilities would risk economic calamity,[^cite_note-graunr-435] Trump signed a [Memorandum of Understanding](83441543.md) (MOU) on behalf of the United States during a ceremony at the [Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles) on 17 June 2026.[^cite_note-graunr-435] According to a report by the *Wall Street Journal*, Trump was initially pleased how the Iran war was going, notably after the killing of Ali Khamenei. However, with increase in fuel prices and a stalemate in the war, leaders from Saudi Arabia and Qatar pushed the US government for a diplomatic solution, while nearly 80% of Americans, according to polls, favored an agreement to end the war, prompting Trump to pursue the June truce.[^cite_note-436]&#x20;

In September 2026, a [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN)-commissioned fact-finding mission panel found "reasonable grounds" to believe that the [United States](3434750.md) had committed war crimes during military strikes in [Iran](14653.md) during the 2026 Iran war. The report highlighted two February 28 strikes that killed at least 178 civilians.[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-ds-un-439][^cite_note-440] U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s threats to completely destroy [Iranian](14653.md) [power plants](212141.md) and other essential infrastructure during the 2026 Iran war have been widely condemned by human rights organizations, legal experts, and lawmakers as potential war crimes.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]&#x20;

##### Israel and Gaza

See also: [United States support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md)



President Trump hosting [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) at the White House on July 7, 2025



Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) hosting Netanyahu at the Pentagon on July 9, 2025

After being elected in November 2024, Trump said he wished to end the [Gaza war](74998519.md),[^cite_note-abces2024-448][^cite_note-449] telling Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) to end [Israel's invasion](74998519.md) within two months[^cite_note-abces2024-448] and warning Hamas that they would have "all hell to pay" if they did not agree to a ceasefire and [release all hostages](75025451.md) by Trump's inauguration.[^cite_note-450] Trump's incoming administration joined the Biden administration in pressuring negotiations,[^cite_note-washingtonpost202501152-451][^cite_note-haaretz202501132-452][^cite_note-nytimes20250115a2-453] and Israel and Hamas [agreed to a phased ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire) on January 15, 2025.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-agreementconfirmed-455] In February 2025, Trump [proposed an American takeover of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal) in which the territory's Palestinian population would be relocated to allow for its redevelopment, which was criticized by Egypt and Jordan.[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-Trump_plan-457] The ceasefire lasted until March 18, when [Israel launched attacks on Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-breaking_ceasefire-458] Trump's special envoy [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) blamed Hamas for renewed fighting in Gaza.[^cite_note-459]&#x20;

On May 16, while in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), United Arab Emirates, Trump said, "We're looking at Gaza. And we're going to get that taken care of. A lot of people are starving." This is an area of tension between President Trump and [Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md). Israel claims that [Hamas](13913.md) systemically raids food aid in order to support its operations, and Israel has put forward a plan of food distributed through a system of hubs run by private contractors and protected by Israeli soldiers. The U.S.-backed [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) has been set up to carry out this plan and announced it would be ready to begin operations by the end of May.[^cite_note-The_Guardian,_Trump_acknowledges_starvation_in_Gaza,_May_16,_2025-460][^cite_note-CBS_News,_Trump_says-461]&#x20;

On July 28, while in [Scotland](26994.md), President Trump said he believed there was [starvation occurring in Gaza](75828824.md). He further added, "Nobody's done anything great over there. The whole place is a mess... I told Israel maybe they have to do it a different way." Israeli Prime Minister said, "And what has interdicted the supply of humanitarian aid is one force, Hamas. Again, the reversal of truth."[^cite_note-BBC,_'real_starvation,'_Trump_says,_July_28,_2025-462][^cite_note-Guardian,_does_not_endorse_Netanyahu's_claim_there_is_no_starvation,_July_28,_2025-463]&#x20;

Asked about an Israeli [double-strike on a hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Nasser_Hospital_strikes) on August 25, 2025, Trump said he was "not happy about it", adding, "We have to end this whole nightmare."[^cite_note-NBC,_Israel_faces_new_pressure_over_hospital_strikes,_August_27,_2025-464]&#x20;



Trump at the [Gaza peace summit](81324529.md) in [Sharm El Sheikh](419335.md), Egypt, on October 13, 2025

On September 29, 2025, President Trump announced, alongside Prime Minister Netanyahu, a [20-Point Gaza Peace Plan](81215079.md) from the White House which consisted of 20 specific points aimed at achieving a ceasefire, the return of [Israeli hostages](75025451.md), dismantling [Hamas](13913.md)'s military capabilities, and establishing a transitional governance structure in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).[^cite_note-465]&#x20;

In October 2025, President Trump announced his deal for a ceasefire between Israel and Hamas had been reached and that the remaining hostages would be released.[^cite_note-466] In exchange, Israel will release 250 Palestinian prisoners serving longterm sentences and another 1,700 other prisoners. The Israeli military will withdraw from a "blue line" to a "yellow line" further away from the Gaza coast.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Israel-Hamas_ceasefire,_Oct_9,_2025-467] Trump was widely praised for negotiating this settlement, including by former presidents Bill Clinton and Barack Obama and former Secretary of State and 2016 presidential candidate Hillary Clinton.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469]&#x20;

##### Qatar



Trump and Qatar's emir [Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani](6146151.md) in [Lusail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusail), Qatar during [Donald Trump's visit to the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2025_visit_by_Donald_Trump_to_the_Middle_East), May 2025

In September 2025, following an Israeli strike against suspected Hamas terrorists in Qatar's capital city of Doha, President Trump signed an executive order which stated, "The United States shall regard any armed attack on the territory, sovereignty, or critical infrastructure of the State of Qatar as a threat to the peace and security of the United States."[^cite_note-CBS_News,_U.S._base_in_Idaho,_Oct_10,_2025-470] President Biden had begun a closer relationship with Qatar following their help with the 2001 American withdrawal from Afghanistan. In January 2022, Biden named Qatar a [major non-NATO ally](3812445.md).[^cite_note-ABC_News,_executive_order_vowing_to_defend_Qatar,_Oct_1,_2025-471]&#x20;

The editorial board of *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* wrote, "This is a decision that can be and should have been debated. Instead it comes out of the blue — an executive order following no public debate."[^cite_note-ABC_News,_executive_order_vowing_to_defend_Qatar,_Oct_1,_2025-471]&#x20;

In October, Defense Secretary Hegseth announced a training agreement that the Qatar Air Force will build a facility at the [Mountain Home Air Force Base](55328338.md) in Idaho. The United States has a similar arrangement with Singapore, at the same base. Hegseth emphasized that the U.S. will remain in charge of the facility "like we do with all partners".[^cite_note-CBS_News,_U.S._base_in_Idaho,_Oct_10,_2025-470]&#x20;

##### Syria



Trump with Syrian president [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) and Crown Prince of Saudi Arabia [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) in [Riyadh](51554.md), Saudi Arabia, May 14, 2025

During the [2024 Syrian opposition offensives](78460726.md) that [toppled the Assad regime](78548066.md) in Syria, Trump said that the U.S. should stay out of the conflict.[^cite_note-472] In March 2025, Rubio condemned the [massacres of Syrian Alawites](79404646.md).[^cite_note-473] Trump agreed to lift sanctions on Syria after discussing the situation with [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) and [Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) in May 2025.[^cite_note-Ghinwa_Obeid-474][^cite_note-Gram_Slattery_et_al.-475]&#x20;

#### Indian subcontinent

Further information: [2025 United States–India diplomatic and trade crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States%E2%80%93India_diplomatic_and_trade_crisis)



Trump with Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md), February 2025

[India](14533.md) rejected Trump's offer to mediate [India–China tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93India_relations).[^cite_note-476] On February 13, 2025, Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) became the fourth world leader to visit Donald Trump at the White House.[^cite_note-477]&#x20;

In early 2025, deportations to Bhutan of Nepali-speaking [Bhutanese refugees](7654172.md) who had been settled in the US for nearly two decades resulted in statelessness of the deportees following their interrogation and expulsion from Bhutan upon arrival. Some of the [refouled refugees](2702190.md), who had encountered largely minor legal issues in the US, were then arrested for illegal entry to Nepal upon seeking to re-enter the refugee camps they had previously inhabited before US resettlement.[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479]&#x20;



Trump with Pakistani prime minister [Shehbaz Sharif](6502561.md) (left) and Pakistani chief of Army staff [Asim Munir](58712552.md) (right)

In response to the [2025 India–Pakistan conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_strikes), Vice President Vance said that, a potential war between India and Pakistan is "none of our business".[^cite_note-480] Trump claimed to have negotiated a ceasefire, a claim that Pakistan corroborated and India denied.[^cite_note-481][^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483]&#x20;

In July 2025, Trump criticized India over its continued oil trade with Russia, despite ongoing Western sanctions.[^cite_note-NDTV2025-484] On July 30, 2025, Trump announced that Indian products would be subject to 25% reciprocal [tariffs](78448911.md) upon arrival in the United States starting August 1, and that a "penalty" would be added for the purchase of Russian oil.[^cite_note-485] On August 6, 2025, Trump raised the tariff to 50%, a 25% increase, over India's purchase of Russian oil.[^cite_note-486]&#x20;

In July 2025, Trump's administration reached a [trade agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan%E2%80%93United_States_trade_deal) with Pakistan, in which Trump announced that the United States would help Pakistan develop its large and untapped [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves).[^cite_note-APNews2025a-487] The agreement also reduced American reciprocal tariffs on Pakistan to 19%, the lowest of any country in [South Asia](21566765.md).[^cite_note-APNews2025a-487]&#x20;

#### Caucasus

Main article: [Armenia–Azerbaijan peace agreement](79518584.md)



Azerbaijani president [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md), President Trump and Armenian prime minister [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md) signing a trilateral joint declaration in Washington, D.C., on August 8, 2025

On April 24, 2025, during the first [Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day) of Trump's second term, the White House issued a statement commemorating the day that did not use the term "genocide".[^cite_note-488] This was seen as a reversal after Joe Biden formally [recognized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_the_Armenian_genocide) the [Armenian genocide](140376.md) in 2021.[^cite_note-489] The second Trump administration continues to avoid using the term "genocide", instead continuing to refer to the events as the *[Medz Yeghern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminology_of_the_Armenian_genocide#Armenian)* ([Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_language) for 'Great Catastrophe') and labeling April 24 as only "Armenian Remembrance Day".[^cite_note-490]&#x20;

On August 8, 2025, Armenian prime minister [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md) and Azerbaijani president [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) signed a [peace agreement](79518584.md) in a ceremony hosted by President Donald Trump in the White House,[^cite_note-491] aiming to end the more than [35-year conflict](4695860.md) between Armenia and Azerbaijan.[^cite_note-492]&#x20;

#### Southeast Asia

In 2025, Trump attempted to negotiate a ceasefire in the [border conflict between Cambodia and Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Cambodia%E2%80%93Thailand_border_conflict).[^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494]&#x20;

#### China

Further information: [China–United States trade war](57055272.md)



Trump meets with [Chinese leader](262766.md) [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) at the [summit meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Busan_Summit) in [Busan](68115.md) on October 30, 2025

In February 2025, Trump signed an executive order that directed the [Committee on Foreign Investment in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_on_Foreign_Investment_in_the_United_States) (CFIUS) to restrict Chinese investment in strategic economic areas.[^cite_note-495]&#x20;

Trump blamed China for the [opioid crisis in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opioid_epidemic_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-496] He said the tariffs are intended to pressure China to do more to stop the flow of [fentanyl](141915.md) into the US.[^cite_note-497] [Opioids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opioid), predominantly fentanyl, have killed over 500,000 Americans since 2012.[^cite_note-498]&#x20;

In September 2025, Trump asked the EU to impose 100% tariffs on China to punish it for [buying Russian oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and de facto financing Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500]&#x20;

### Oceania

#### Australia



Trump greets Australian Prime Minister [Anthony Albanese](556745.md) at the White House, October 20, 2025

On October 21, 2025, partly as a consequence of the [China–U.S. trade war](57055272.md) that included China imposing restrictions on its sale of processed [rare-earth elements](145440.md), Trump signed a deal with [Anthony Albanese](556745.md), Prime Minister of Australia,[^cite_note-sherman2025-501] over rare-earths and other [critical minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critical_minerals)[^cite_note-503] that are needed for commercial [clean energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_energy) production and technologically advanced [military hardware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_hardware). They each committed to provide at least US\$1bn (A\$1.54bn) towards a number of projects worth \$US8.5bn (A\$13bn) in both countries over six months.[^cite_note-barrett-504][^cite_note-govtannounce-505] The deal has been well received by the [Australian rare-earths industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_rare-earths_industry)[^cite_note-li2025-506][^cite_note-wood2025-507] and the markets,[^cite_note-capolingua-508] and is important to the U.S. because rare earths are used in many technologies, including components of the Defense Force such as [F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) fighter jets and [Tomahawk missiles](31238.md).[^cite_note-509] The deal is also seen as a major shift in economic alliances.[^cite_note-capolingua-508]&#x20;

### Foreign aid

Main article: [United States Agency for International Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development)

#### Freezing most programs for 90 days

DOGE dismantled most of [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID),[^cite_note-Knickmeyer2025-510] which had been a 10,000-person agency originally tasked to carry-out humanitarian projects.[^cite_note-511] USAID's critics maintained that many of the projects were in fact not all that humanitarian and/or much of the money was not well spent.[^cite_note-512]&#x20;

In January 2025, the administration issued a 90-day stop-work order worldwide,[^cite_note-AP_News,_State_Department_freezes_new_funding,_January_24,_2025-513][^cite_note-ProPublica-Jan-514] Stop work interrupted about 30 [clinical trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinical_trial),[^cite_note-515] and interrupted projects such as emergency medical care for displaced Palestinians and Yemenis, aid for war refugees on the Sudan-Chad border, and electricity for Ukrainian refugees.[^cite_note-ProPublica-Jan-514] The HIV Modeling Consortium estimated the death toll from HIV in sub-Saharan Africa at approximately 15,000 adults and 1,600 children in the one month after Trump's January 2025 funding freeze.[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517] A key controversy is whether or not waivers are being made and money actually starting to flow again for the most essential programs.[^cite_note-ProPublica-Jan-514] For example, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) reported on February 19, that waivers for [PEPFAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEPFAR), the program that has saved 26 million lives from [AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIDS), were not in force, despite a federal judge having lifted the funding freeze.[^cite_note-518]&#x20;



U.S. Army and Air Force service members assisted USAID with the transportation of nearly 12,000 pounds of medical kits from the Netherlands, through Germany and on to Lebanon in August 2020.

In early February 2025, it was reported that the [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) director of security and a deputy were put on administrative leave after they "blocked efforts by DOGE members to physically access restricted areas" in order to obtain sensitive information. The DOGE members eventually gained access to the information, which reportedly included email as well as classified information for which they did not have security clearance. And they obtained the ability to lock USAID staff out. Musk had earlier tweeted "USAID is a criminal organization" and that it is "time for it to die".[^cite_note-519] Estimates do vary. [Oxfam](19698439.md) criticized Musk's policies and actions as the world's richest individual deciding "to shutter the world's largest provider of lifesaving humanitarian aid".[^cite_note-520]&#x20;

USAID staff were instructed to keep away from USAID headquarters while hundreds of USAID staff lost access to USAID computer systems.[^cite_note-Knickmeyer2025-510]&#x20;

There have been reports that China has offered to take over development projects if the United States permanently leaves.[^cite_note-CNN,_Trump_says_dismantling,_Feb_7,_2025-521][^cite_note-Politico,_As_USAID,_Feb_10,_2025-522][^cite_note-NBC_News,_Cambodian_mine-clearing,_Feb_14,_2025-523]&#x20;

During a February 6 press conference, [Secretary of State Marco Rubio](5502549.md) stated, "If it's providing food or medicine or anything that is saving lives and is immediate and urgent, you're not included in the freeze."[^cite_note-CNN,_State_Department_says_humanitarian,_February_8,_2025-524] However, a February 8 CNN article reported that many waivers were not being acted upon because of staff placed on leave, plus payment systems had been taken over.[^cite_note-CNN,_State_Department_says_humanitarian,_February_8,_2025-524] In an interview before the [February 9 Super Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX), Trump said, "Let him take care of the few good ones", referring to Rubio.[^cite_note-AP_News,_Unspent_aid_worth_billions,_Feb_11,_2035-525][^cite_note-526]&#x20;

On February 13, federal judge [Amir Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Ali_\(judge\)) ordered the Trump administration to continue contracts and grants which were in effect January 19.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Judge_orders_Trump_administration_to_temporarily_restore_funding,_Feb_13,_2025-527] Chief Justice John Roberts, overseeing cases for the District of Columbia, paused this order.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Chief_Justice_halts,_Feb_27,_2025-528][^cite_note-Reuters,_Foreign_aid_groups_ask,_Feb_28,_2025-529] On March 5, the Supreme Court ruled 5–4 that the Trump administration must comply with Judge Ali's order.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Supreme_Court_won't_lift_lower_court_order,_March_5,_2025-530][^cite_note-531] However, the Supreme Court stated that Judge Ali must clarify the payment obligations with "due regard for the feasibility of any compliance timelines".[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Supreme_Court_won't_lift_lower_court_order,_March_5,_2025-530] On March 10, Judge Ali ruled that the Trump administration must pay for completed projects at the rate of 300 back payments a day, meaning four days for all 1,200 back payments, and this being for projects completed by February 13.[^cite_note-Axios,_Judge_holds_Congress_has_power,_March_11,_2025-532][^cite_note-ABC_&_Associated_Press,_Trump_overstepped,_March_11,_2025-533] A March 11 [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) article reported that, until recently, no payments were being made because DOGE had disabled the payment system.[^cite_note-ABC_&_Associated_Press,_Trump_overstepped,_March_11,_2025-533]&#x20;

#### Keeping 17% of programs

On March 10, Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) announced that he was cancelling 83% of USAID programs, or approximately 5,200 out of 6,200 programs. The remaining 1,000 programs (approximately) would be moved to the Department of State.[^cite_note-534][^cite_note-CBS_News,_Secretary_of_state_says_83%,_March_10,_2025-535] As of late March, DOGE no longer lists the details of canceled USAID contracts on its "Wall of Receipts".[^cite_note-CBS_News,_DOGE_removes_details,_Mar_26,_2025-536]&#x20;

DOGE lists approximately \$12 billion saved, although a former USAID analyst estimates the actual amount is closer to \$6 or \$7 billion.[^cite_note-CBS_News,_DOGE_removes_details,_Mar_26,_2025-536]&#x20;

In early April, USAID announced it was adding back 14 nations to grants under the UN's [World Food Programme](33582.md). These nations include Lebanon, Syria, Somalia, Jordan, Iraq and Ecuador, plus the [International Organization for Migration](36893.md) in the Pacific region.[^cite_note-Reuters,_Trump_administration_moves,_April_8,_2025-537] However, food aid was not restored to either Yemen or Afghanistan, with a State Department spokesperson saying this was "based on concern that the funding was benefiting terrorist groups, including the Houthis and the Taliban".[^cite_note-CNN,_USAID_reverses_course,_April_9,_2025-538]&#x20;

#### July 2025 rescissions ("claw backs"), but not for PEPFAR

In June 2025, the White House requested that Congress pass a package of [rescissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rescission_bill), or "claw backs", of approximately \$8 billion in foreign aid and \$1 billion for the [Corporation for Public Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporation_for_Public_Broadcasting) including [NPR](29697232.md) and [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS).[^cite_note-CBS,_Trump's_request_to_cancel_billions,_July_15,_2025-539]&#x20;

The House of Representatives passed the cuts as requested. The Senate excluded the [PEPFAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEPFAR) cuts, which is the program started in 2003 during the [presidency of George W Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush) to help provide HIV medicines to lower-income countries. The Senate passed two preliminary votes in close 51-50 fashion with [Vice President JD Vance](53396477.md) casting the tie-breaking votes. A rescission is one of the exceptions to the Senate's 60-vote [filibuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filibuster_in_the_United_States_Senate) rule.[^cite_note-CBS,_Trump's_request_to_cancel_billions,_July_15,_2025-539]&#x20;

President Trump focused on the public broadcasting aspect, criticizing CNN and also "MSDNC" which is a mix of [MSNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSNBC) and [DNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_National_Committee) (Democratic National Committee). In a [Truth Social](69077351.md) post, he wrote, "It is very important that all Republicans adhere to my Recissions Bill and, in particular, DEFUND THE CORPORATION FOR PUBLIC BROADCASTING (PBS and NPR), which is worse than CNN & MSDNC put together."[^cite_note-ABC,_Senate_to_vote_to_formalize,_July_14,_2025-540]&#x20;

In a late night session at 2\:00 AM on July 17, the Senate voted 51–48 in favor of the cuts. Later that same day, the House of Representatives voted 216 - 213 for the Senate version, meaning PEPFAR was protected in the amount of \$400 million.[^cite_note-ABC,_Senate_to_vote_to_formalize,_July_14,_2025-540] Regarding the cuts to public broadcasting, service to rural areas became one of the political issues.[^cite_note-NBC,_Trump's_cuts_clear_Congress,_July_17,_2025-541]&#x20;

### Disaster response

#### Myanmar earthquake, March 2025

President Trump stated the United States would help in response to the [March 28 earthquake in Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Myanmar_earthquake), although the question was asked by a former USAID executive of whether or not the United States will beat other countries in getting there with a relief team(s).[^cite_note-542]&#x20;

#### Hurricane Melissa, October 2025

[Hurricane Melissa](81393820.md) was a [Category 5](255313.md) [hurricane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane) which first made landfall at [Jamaica](15660.md) on October 28, 2025, and then hit other [Caribbean islands](18956035.md). [Secretary of State Rubio](5502549.md) activated a disaster assistance response team. By October 31, three U.S. [CH-47 Chinook helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_CH-47_Chinook) were deployed to Jamaica to help with relief efforts, with five more on the way.[^cite_note-543] In early November, the State Department promised \$12 million in aid to Jamaica, \$8 million to [Haiti](13373.md), \$3 million to [Cuba](5042481.md), and \$500,000 to [The Bahamas](3451.md).[^cite_note-544]&#x20;

### Refugees

On January 20, 2025, Trump signed the executive order named "Securing Our Borders". This order suspends [the Uniting for Ukraine parole program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parole_\(United_States_immigration\)#Uniting_for_Ukraine), and no new applications from [Ukrainian refugees](70199806.md) with relatives in the United States are being accepted.[^cite_note-545][^cite_note-546]&#x20;

On May 12, 59 [white South African refugees arrived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_South_African_refugee_program) from South Africa. The Trump administration has been criticized for fast-tracking their applications while pausing other refugee programs.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_First_group_of_49_white_South_Africans,_May_11,_2025-547]&#x20;

The Trump administration slowed the entry of [Afghan refugees](3657017.md) into the U.S., including persons who had fought on the U.S. side against the Taliban. A leader of an American veterans group said, "these pilots risked everything for America. Their lives are now on the line because of our failure to follow through on our promises."[^cite_note-CNN_News,_Afghan_pilots_who_fought_in_20-year_war_against_Taliban_in_limbo,_March_28,_2025-548]&#x20;

On May 12, [Department of Homeland Security](58236.md) [Secretary Noem](27663933.md) announced that [TPS](20497716.md), or [temporary protected status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temporary_Protected_Status), for Afghan refugees would be ended in 60 days, which is the shortest time period permitted by United States law. This decision cited improved conditions in Afghanistan.[^cite_note-The_Hill,_Trump_administration_ends_protections_from_deportation_for_Afghans,_May_12,_2025-549]&#x20;

*[The Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hill_\(newspaper\))* reports that the [UN high commissioner for refugees](31964.md) has stated that the situation in Afghanistan has not improved, with a report stating "The large-scale returns are putting even greater pressure on already stretched humanitarian resources." On the other hand, Secretary Noem has pointed to an increase in tourists from China and a drop in the number of Afghans needing assistance from 29 million to 24 million. [^cite_note-The_Hill,_Trump_administration_ends_protections_from_deportation_for_Afghans,_May_12,_2025-549]&#x20;

[Fox News](11121.md) reported that the leader of "Afghans for Trump", which formed in the aftermath of President Biden's chaotic 2021 withdrawal from Afghanistan, claimed that conditions have not significantly improved and urged President Trump to reconsider.[^cite_note-Fox_News,_'Afghans_for_Trump'_group_feels_abandoned,_May_16,_2025-550]&#x20;

### Expansionism and revanchism

Main article: [American expansionism under Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump)



Trump with NATO general secretary [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) at the [2025 The Hague NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit)

In the lead-up to his second inauguration, Trump proposed plans and ideas that would expand the United States' political influence and territory.[^cite_note-551] It has been characterized as [a revival of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donroe_Doctrine) the [Monroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe_Doctrine).[^cite_note-552] The [last territory acquired by the United States was in 1947](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_territorial_acquisitions_table) when the [Mariana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariana_Islands), [Caroline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Islands), and [Marshall Islands](19147.md) were acquired.&#x20;

#### Canada

See also: [Movements for the annexation of Canada to the United States § Proposals to annex Canada by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movements_for_the_annexation_of_Canada_to_the_United_States#Proposals_to_annex_Canada_by_Donald_Trump)



Trump with Canadian prime minister [Mark Carney](13571049.md), June 2025

The Trump administration has imposed 25% tariffs on Canadian goods imports that do not fall under [Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada%E2%80%93United_States%E2%80%93Mexico_Agreement), with exceptions for steel, aluminum, and Canadian-made passenger vehicle content.[^cite_note-553] Canadian officials responded with retaliatory 25% tariffs,[^cite_note-554][^cite_note-555] and have even proposed cutting off the supply of Canadian energy into the [Northern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_United_States).[^cite_note-556] [Manitoba](18926.md) has diverted energy to Canada that it previously supplied to the USA.[^cite_note-557] [Peter Navarro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Navarro), a trade envoy and high-ranking Trump official, has suggested expelling Canada from the [Five Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Eyes) intelligence alliance to increase pressure in the trade war.[^cite_note-p983-558]&#x20;

Trump has repeatedly stated his desire for the United States to annex Canada and calling for it to become the 51st state,[^cite_note-d293-559] calling former Canadian prime minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) "Governor of the Great State of Canada".[^cite_note-560] Trudeau stated to business leaders in Canada that he believes the annexation threats are a "real thing".[^cite_note-s944-561] The strained relations have led to a "Buy Canadian" movement to boycott American goods and services,[^cite_note-u378-562] and booing the American national anthem during international hockey games.[^cite_note-t749-563] When tariffs were implemented on March 4, Trudeau stated that Trump's goal in the trade war was to weaken Canada in order to annex it.[^cite_note-g713-564]&#x20;
