On February 8, 2026, Trump threatened to block the opening of the [Gordie Howe International Bridge](26342191.md), citing Canada's recent trade developments with China and the alleged one-sided nature of the contract (wherein Canada gains all revenue from bridge tolls to pay back construction costs). Trump said the bridge's opening will be delayed until multiple trade and bridge related grievances were resolved. Trump's announcement was criticised by politicians, business leaders and industry groups.[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566][^cite_note-567]&#x20;

On February 12, 2026, the United States House of Representatives voted to repeal the tariffs that had been imposed on Canadian goods during Donald Trump's administration. The resolution passed by a 219–211 vote, with a small number of Republican lawmakers joining nearly all Democrats in support. The tariffs had originally been enacted under a national emergency declaration in February 2025, and their repeal represented a rare bipartisan rebuke of Trump's trade policy. Following the vote, President Trump reportedly threatened political consequences for Republicans who voted against the measure. While passage in the House reflects congressional concern over the tariffs, actual repeal would still require Senate approval and presidential assent, and was expected to face a potential veto.[^cite_note-568][^cite_note-569][^cite_note-570]&#x20;

#### Greenland

See also: [Greenland crisis](82044223.md) and [Proposals for the United States to purchase Greenland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposals_for_the_United_States_to_purchase_Greenland)



Greenlandic leader [Jens Frederik-Nielsen](79438070.md) announcing "We choose Denmark" at a January 2026 press conference with [Mette Frederiksen](26040044.md) in response to [Trump's threats to invade or annex](82044223.md) the country

[^cite_note-571]

[^cite_note-572]

In December 2024, Trump stated a further [proposal for the United States to purchase Greenland from Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposals_for_the_United_States_to_purchase_Greenland), describing "ownership" and control of the island as "an absolute necessity" for national security purposes. This builds upon a prior offer from Trump to buy Greenland during his first term, which the [Danish Realm](11746442.md) refused, causing him to cancel his August 2019 visit to Denmark.[^cite_note-573] On January 7, 2025, Trump's son [Donald Trump Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_Jr.) visited Greenland's capital city [Nuuk](53303.md) alongside [Charlie Kirk](53245776.md) to hand out [MAGA hats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MAGA_hat).[^cite_note-574] At a press conference the following day, Trump refused to rule out military or economic force to take over Greenland or the Panama Canal.[^cite_note-Davies-2025-575] However, he did rule out military force in taking over Canada.[^cite_note-Davies-2025-575] On January 14, the Trump-affiliated [Nelk Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelk) also visited Nuuk, handing out [dollar bills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Note) to locals.[^cite_note-576] On January 16, the CEOs of major Danish companies Novo Nordisk, [Vestas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vestas) and [Carlsberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlsberg_Group) among others were assembled for a crisis meeting in the [Ministry of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_State_\(Denmark\)) to discuss the situation.[^cite_note-577][^cite_note-578]&#x20;

On the subsequent day, former chief executive [Friis Arne Petersen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friis_Arne_Petersen) in the Danish [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Denmark\)) described the situation as "historically unheard of", while [Noa Redington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noa_Redington), special adviser to former prime minister [Helle Thorning-Schmidt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helle_Thorning-Schmidt), compared the international pressure on Denmark that during the 2005 [*Jyllands-Posten* Muhammad cartoons controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten_Muhammad_cartoons_controversy).[^cite_note-579] On February 12, 2025, a bill was introduced in Congress to advance efforts to acquire Greenland and rename it Red, White, and Blueland.[^cite_note-580] Trump's actions against Greenland have been described as [hybrid warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_warfare) by academics and analysts.[^cite_note-dab-581] Prime Minister [Mette Frederiksen](26040044.md) said that "the Kingdom of Denmark—and thus Greenland—is a member of NATO and is therefore covered by the Alliance's [collective security guarantee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty#Article_5) ... I ... strongly urge the United States to cease its threats against a historically close ally."[^cite_note-mefr-582] The official Danish threat assessment published by the [Danish Defence Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Defence_Intelligence_Service) in 2025 for the first time mentioned the [United States](3434750.md) as a threat to [national security](240468.md), alongside [Russia](25391.md) and [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-politico-583][^cite_note-584][^cite_note-585] In 2026 Greenland and Denmark saw massive [anti-Trump protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Trump_protests), the [Hands off Greenland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hands_off_Greenland) protests, where protesters chanted "Greenland is not for sale".[^cite_note-586][^cite_note-587][^cite_note-588]&#x20;

#### Panama Canal

In 2024, Trump demanded that [Panama](22997.md) return control of the [Panama Canal](24849.md) to the United States because "excessive rates" were being charged for American passage.[^cite_note-589] The United States previously was in control of the [Panama Canal Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_Canal_Zone) from [1903](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hay%E2%80%93Bunau-Varilla_Treaty) until [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torrijos%E2%80%93Carter_Treaties), and has [invaded Panama before in 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Panama).[^cite_note-590][^cite_note-591] Trump told Congress in March 2025 that his administration "will be reclaiming the Panama Canal".[^cite_note-592] Also that month, Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth instructed the Trump administration to "immediately" present "credible military options to ensure fair and unfettered US military and commercial access to the Panama Canal".[^cite_note-593]&#x20;

### United Nations



Trump and other leaders during the [Board of Peace](81274522.md) meeting in Davos, Switzerland, February 2026

On July 22, 2025, the United States informed of its decision to withdraw from [UNESCO](21786641.md), effective December 31, 2026.[^cite_note-594][^cite_note-us_withdrawal-595][^cite_note-596]&#x20;

In a presidential memorandum issued on January 7, 2026, Donald Trump directed to start the process of withdrawal of the US from the 66 organizations, agencies and commissions of the [United Nations](31769.md), including [UN Framework Convention on Climate Change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Framework_Convention_on_Climate_Change) (UNFCCC), calling them "contrary to the interests of the United States".[^cite_note-WH-597][^cite_note-598]&#x20;

#### World Health Organization

On January 22, 2026, President Donald Trump completed the withdrawal of the United States from the [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO), a specialized agency of the United Nations, a year after he signed [Executive Order 14155](78942315.md) on January 20, 2025. As announced by the [Department of Health and Human Services](58250.md), the United States officially exited the organization, ending its 78-year membership and halting all U.S. funding and participation in the WHO's governance and activities.[^cite_note-HHS-599] Although the organization hoped for a reconsideration the day after Trump's executive order,[^cite_note-600] the administration continued with the decision.&#x20;

The administration justified the withdrawal by citing concerns about the WHO's handling of global health emergencies, including the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), its resistance to reforms, and its failure to operate independently from inappropriate political influence of other WHO member states.[^cite_note-HHS-599] In a joint statement by [Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_state_\(U.S._state_government\)) [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) and [Secretary of Health and Human Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_Health_and_Human_Services) [Robert F. Kennedy Jr.](522298.md), they said that the WHO "tarnished and trashed everything that America has done for it" and it "abandoned its core mission and acted repeatedly against the interests of the United States", including the failure to "hand over the American flag that hung in front of it".[^cite_note-601]&#x20;

The United States still owes outstanding dues to the WHO from 2024 to 2025, raising questions about the legality and financial implications of the exit.[^cite_note-602] Being one of the biggest donors to the WHO, the country's unpaid fees of estimated \$260 million has already caused massive job losses at the organization. However, as a response, Washington said they saw no reason to comply.[^cite_note-603]&#x20;

Ronald G. Nahass, president of the [Infectious Diseases Society of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infectious_Diseases_Society_of_America) (IDSA), criticized the withdrawal, calling it "a shortsighted and misguided abandonment of our global health commitments" and "scientifically reckless". He emphasized that "It fails to acknowledge the fundamental natural history of infectious diseases" and that "Global cooperation and communication are critical to keep our own citizens protected because germs do not respect borders," adding that global cooperation is "not a luxury" but a "biological necessity".[^cite_note-604]&#x20;

## Ethics

See also: [First presidency of Donald Trump § Ethics](52231341.md#Ethics)

### Corruption allegations

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Corruption allegations during the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency\&action=edit)]

Trump's second presidency has faced unprecedented conflicts of interest, self-enrichment, and allegations of [corruption](3608404.md) unrivaled in American history from historians, legal experts, and federal judges.[^cite_note-626]&#x20;

His administration has faced widespread [allegations of insider trading](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insider_trading_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_administration) and raised conflict of interest concerns due to business dealings by Trump's son-in-law [Jared Kushner](6191053.md) and his cabinet.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_theguardian.com-627][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_nytimes.com-628][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Lipton_212025-629] Trump's grants of [pardons and clemency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_granted_executive_clemency_in_the_second_Trump_presidency) have favored political allies and loyalists, with several given after millions in donations or fees.[^cite_note-630][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_axios.com-631][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Thrush_May292025-632] Trump has proactively solicited donations from companies and individuals following which favorable regulatory actions and decisions have occurred.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Dawsey_08102026-609] Trump has maintained an active stock market account with thousands of trades totaling hundreds of millions of dollars.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_nbcnews.com-633] Trump has directly benefited by soliciting his own memecoin [\$TRUMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\$TRUMP) and benefitted from his family's cryptocurrency company [World Liberty Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Liberty_Financial) which has received investments by anonymous individuals and foreign governments.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Lipton_04292025-634] As of his July 2026 financial report, Trump made over \$2.2B in 2025 compared to \$622M in 2024, of which \$1.4B came from his family's cryptocurrency business.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Protess_06302026-635] Trump has received an unusually large number of expensive gifts, such as a \$400M [jet from Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_VC-25B_Bridge) or gold bars, which some scholars view as a violation of the emoluments clause of the Constitution.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_npr.org-636][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_thetimes.com-637]&#x20;

Trump's second term has been described by political commentators as having fewer prohibitions on business activity and guardrails against potential conflicts of interest than his first, and for having more opportunities to directly influence Trump.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Yourish_2025-638][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Schouten_2025-639] Trump repealed and rolled back anti-corruption measures and ethical standards for himself and his allies, dropped corruption charges against political figures with ties to him, and [fired inspectors generals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_dismissals_of_U.S._inspectors_general) investigating fraud and abuse. *The New York Times* described Trump as making up statistics "out of thin air", and for accusing government agencies and "anyone he disfavors of corruption and even criminality without proof".[^cite_note-640] His second presidency was described as breaking with decades of ethical norms,[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Lipton_02172025-641] and raising substantial corruption concerns.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Savage_05122025-642][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Stringer_05182025-610] Congressional Republicans have largely downplayed or ignored the concerns.[^cite_note-643][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Lipton_02172025-641]&#x20;

Federal judges found many of the administration's actions to be illegal and unconstitutional,[^cite_note-650] and by mid-July, a *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)* analysis found he defied judges and the courts in roughly one third of all cases against him, actions which were described by legal experts as unprecedented for any presidential administration.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Jouvenal_07212025-651] His defiance of court orders and a claimed right to disobey the courts raised fears among legal experts of a [constitutional crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_crisis).[^cite_note-652] An August 2026 [Reuters](18998750.md)/[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll found a majority of Americans believe that Trump and his family "have inappropriately profited from cryptocurrency since his return to power and that his policy decisions are influenced by his private business dealings".[^cite_note-653]&#x20;

### Alleged conflict of interest

In September 2026, a Powerus delegation met General [Asim Munir](58712552.md) and a [MoU](1018279.md) was signed between the Pakistan Army and the Trump-family backed drone maker while the company was due to merge with Aureus Greenway Holdings linked to Eric Trump and Donald Trump Jr. However, Andrew Fox, Powerus' founder, denied that "the company's growth was linked to the involvement of Trump's sons."[^cite_note-654][^cite_note-655]&#x20;

### Pardons and commutations

Further information: [List of people granted executive clemency in the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_granted_executive_clemency_in_the_second_Trump_presidency)

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Corruption allegations during the second Trump presidency § Pardons and commutations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency#Pardons_and_commutations).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency\&action=edit)]

As of November 2025 Trump had issued nearly two thousand pardons and commutations[^cite_note-656] including for some prosecuted during his first presidency.[^cite_note-657] His grant of pardons outpaced those of prior presidents.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Ava_08102026-658] Trump's pardons and grants of clemency favored political allies and loyalists.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_axios.com-631][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Thrush_May292025-632] Lobbyists have told the Wall Street Journal and other news outlets that fees of \$1M are standard. Some would-be pardon recipients have offered success fees of \$6M for a successful application.[^cite_note-659][^cite_note-660][^cite_note-661]&#x20;

Trump frequently bypassed the [Office of the Pardon Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Pardon_Attorney), and on March 7, 2025, fired its leader, Department of Justice career attorney Liz Oyer, and installed political loyalist [Ed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Martin_\(Missouri_politician\)) in the role.[^cite_note-662][^cite_note-663] Ed Martin described the rationale for granting pardons as "No MAGA left behind".[^cite_note-664] In April 2025, Oyer testified to the Senate and accused the Justice Department of "ongoing corruption" and that "the leadership of the Department of Justice appears to value political loyalty above the fair and responsible administration of justice".[^cite_note-665] In 2025, Trump pardoned 42 people charged with white-collar crimes. As of late January 2026, he had pardoned an additional 10 white collar criminals.[^cite_note-666] In April 2026, [The Wall Street Journal](173070.md) reported that Trump "repeatedly promised his top administration officials pardons before he leaves office".[^cite_note-667] In June 2026, a Reuters investigation found 96% of clemency grants failed to meet longstanding Department of Justice guidelines for such requests, that they were based on partisan and personal criteria, and that there was evidence of payments being used to solicit them.[^cite_note-668]&#x20;

Trump was the first president in modern American history to issue pardons for businesses, and by July 2026 had issued nine and wiped out \$200 million in penalties, some of which was destined for victims of wrongdoing.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Ava_08102026-658] Bloomberg highlighted the case of HDR Global Trading, which owned crypto platform [BitMEX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BitMEX) and was the first company pardoned instead of a [natural person](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_person).[^cite_note-669][^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Ava_08102026-658] On January 15, 2025, BitMEX was fined \$100 million for violating anti-money laundering laws after pleading guilty in July 2024.[^cite_note-670][^cite_note-671] That month, the exchange launched a swap listing tied to the [\$TRUMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\$TRUMP) memecoin and its founder penned an essay which Bloomberg described as "framing the president's embrace of meme coins as a move toward bolstering political accountability". On March 27, 2025, Trump signed pardons for four BitMEX executives and pardoned the company the same week the \$100 million fine was due.[^cite_note-Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency_Ava_08102026-658]&#x20;

### Branding of institutions

Main article: [Branding of United States government programs and facilities after Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branding_of_United_States_government_programs_and_facilities_after_Donald_Trump)

Trump, long known before he took elective office for labeling his companies' properties and merchandise with his name or image,[^cite_note-672] has controversially continued the practice in public life in his second presidential term, in some cases by altering existing names or images.&#x20;

Soon after the term began, Trump's portrait appeared on large banners hung from three federal buildings in Washington, D.C.; U.S. Sen. [Adam Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Schiff) (D.-Calif.) objected that the banners violated “a longstanding legal prohibition against the use of federal funds for propaganda and self-aggrandizement purposes.”[^cite_note-673] Trump also promptly dismissed the [Kennedy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts)'s board of trustees, then selected a replacement board, which voted in December to rename the center the Donald J. Trump and the John F. Kennedy Memorial Center for the Performing Arts; its signage was altered to reflect the new name. The change drew criticism from politicians in Congress, some of them in Trump's own party; House [minority leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority_leader) [Hakeem Jeffries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Jeffries) (D-N.Y.) declared that the board had “no authority to actually rename the Kennedy Center in the absence of legislative action,”[^cite_note-674] and a federal judge later ordered that Trump's name be removed.[^cite_note-675] The new board had appointed Trump himself to its chairmanship, a first for a president.[^cite_note-676] That month, Trump also set his name on the [U.S. Institute of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Institute_of_Peace) (USIP), renaming it the Donald J. Trump Institute of Peace, and its signage, too, was so altered. Trump had recently fired not only the USIP's board but its every employee, turning control of its headquarters over to the [General Services Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Services_Administration).[^cite_note-677] Trump further announced that month a plan for a new fleet of [naval](20518076.md) vessels, to be called [Trump-class battleships](81910509.md), although battleships have long been obsolete in naval warfare[^cite_note-678][^cite_note-679] and the Navy's last one in service was decommissioned in 1992.[^cite_note-680]&#x20;

For many years, the [National Park Service](161535.md)'s [annual general pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_the_Beautiful_Pass) depicted natural landscapes, but the new 2026 pass design for U.S. residents has a photo of Donald Trump.[^cite_note-681] The redesign triggered a popular backlash of passholders' covering his photo with stickers; the Park Service responded by updating its ban on defaced passes to include anything obscuring the front.[^cite_note-682] The year also marked the [Treasury](53667.md)'s announcement that Trump's own signature would replace that of the [treasurer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasurer_of_the_United_States) on paper currency, another first for a president;[^cite_note-683] the [State Department](31975.md)’s issuance of a new passport design with a portrait of Trump, the first living president to appear on a U.S. passport, offered as an option to in-person applicants at the Washington, D.C., Passport Agency;[^cite_note-684][^cite_note-685] and Florida Gov. [Ron DeSantis](36729527.md)'s consent to renaming [Palm Beach International Airport](287471.md) after Trump,[^cite_note-686] a change the president had reportedly sought in 2020.[^cite_note-687] Other measures bearing Trump’s name are [Trump accounts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_account), tax-advantaged investment accounts for American minors rolled out as part of the [One Big Beautiful Bill Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Big_Beautiful_Bill_Act); [TrumpRx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TrumpRx), a prescription drug website run by the federal government; and the [Trump Gold Card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Gold_Card), a program offering foreign nationals expedited U.S. residency in exchange for donations of \$1 million or more to the [Commerce Department](70243.md) and denounced by U.S. Sen. [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) (D.-Ill.) as illegal.[^cite_note-688]&#x20;

In September 2025, Trump announced a widespread crackdown on left-wing groups and donors, saying that "the radicals on the left are the problem" with [political violence](73757192.md).

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20250912-689]

[^cite_note-690]

&#x20;However, cumulatively over decades, most extremist killings in the US have been caused by right-wing perpetrators.

[^cite_note-Time_and_Cato-691]

[^cite_note-AP_20250914-692]

### Government targeting of political opponents and civil society

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Targeting of political opponents and civil society under the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration\&action=edit)]

During [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s second presidency, the Trump administration took a series of actions using the government to target his political opponents and [civil society](209443.md). News outlets described his actions as part of his promised "retribution" and "revenge" campaign, within the context of a strongly personalist and leader-centered conception of politics.[^cite_note-693][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Collinson_1282025-694][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Dreisbach_04282025-695][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Moynihan-696] During [his 2024 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign), Trump repeatedly stated that he had "every right" to go after his political opponents.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Byron_1222025_1-697]&#x20;

He undertook a massive expansion of presidential power under a maximalist interpretation of the [unitary executive theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_executive_theory).[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Economist_04242025-698] Several of his actions ignored or violated federal laws, regulations, and the [Constitution](31644.md) according to American legal scholars,[^cite_note-699][^cite_note-700][^cite_note-701] the [Government Accountability Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Accountability_Office),[^cite_note-702] and federal judges.[^cite_note-703][^cite_note-704][^cite_note-705] He threatened, signed executive actions against, and ordered investigations into his political opponents, critics, and organizations aligned with the [Democratic Party](5043544.md).[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Vogel_&_Goldmacher_2025-706] He politicized the [civil service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_federal_civil_service),[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Economist_04242025-698] undertaking [mass layoffs of government employees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_federal_mass_layoffs) to recruit workers more loyal to himself.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Basu_01272025-707] He ended the post-[Watergate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watergate_scandal) norm of [Justice Department](52563.md) independence, weaponizing it and ordering it to target his political enemies.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Savage_04302025-708] He utilized several government agencies to retaliate against his political enemies and continued filing personal lawsuits against his political opponents, companies, and news organizations that angered him.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Dreisbach_04282025-695][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Cooper_09062025-709]&#x20;

By July 2025, Trump had extracted more than \$1.2 billion in settlements in a "cultural crackdown" against a variety of institutions that largely chose to settle rather than fight back.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Basu_07302025-710] He engaged in an unprecedented [targeting of law firms and lawyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeting_of_law_firms_and_lawyers_under_the_second_Trump_administration) that previously represented positions adverse to himself.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Lucas_2025-711][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Scarcella,_Merken_&_Sloan_2025-712] Trump [targeted higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_policy_of_the_second_Donald_Trump_administration#Actions_against_universities) by demanding it give federal oversight of curriculum and [targeted activists](79561481.md), legal immigrants, tourists, and students with visas who expressed criticism of his policies or engaged in pro-Palestinian advocacy.[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Kanno-Youngs-2025-713] He also [detained and deported United States citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deportation_and_detention_of_American_citizens_in_the_second_Trump_administration).[^cite_note-714]&#x20;

Trump's actions against [civil society](209443.md) have been described by legal experts and political scientists as [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism_during_the_Trump_presidencies) and contributing to [democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Riccardi_2025-715][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Leingang-2025-716][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Mascaro-2025-717] as well as negatively impacting [freedom of the press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_government_attacks_on_journalists_in_the_United_States#2025%E2%80%93present:_Donald_Trump's_second_presidency),[^cite_note-718][^cite_note-719][^cite_note-720] [free speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_speech), and the [rule of law](25166191.md).[^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Savage_04302025-708][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Abels_04292025-721][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Hall_04242025-722][^cite_note-Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration_Gilliand_04262025-723]&#x20;

In February 2026, during his State of the Union address to Congress, Trump was publicly criticized by Representatives [Ilhan Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilhan_Omar) and [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib), who challenged his immigration policies and accused his administration of harming U.S. citizens. In response, Trump posted on his social media platform Truth Social, suggesting that the lawmakers "should be sent back from where they came". Omar, a naturalized U.S. citizen born in [Somalia](27358.md), and Tlaib, a U.S.-born citizen of Palestinian descent, were both widely reported to have been the focus of these remarks. Media outlets and civil rights organizations described Trump's comments as xenophobic and controversial, drawing bipartisan criticism for targeting members of Congress based on their heritage.[^cite_note-724][^cite_note-725][^cite_note-726][^cite_note-727]&#x20;

### Flood-the-zone strategy

Main article: [Flood the zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flood_the_zone)



Comparisons between numbers of first-day executive orders

The beginning of Trump's second presidency is largely recognized as the one theorized by political strategist [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon) during Trump's first presidency, here deployed by [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller). The strategy consists of "flood\[ing] the zone of shit", with an impressive number of claims and executive orders with the intent to tire journalists, political opponents and judicial institutions. As such the latter can't react rapidly and appropriately enough, and it nips controversies in the bud because of the flood of information. The administration had already prepared diverse executive orders to allow the signing of 55 executive orders in 20 days, a frequency never before seen.[^cite_note-728][^cite_note-729][^cite_note-730][^cite_note-731][^cite_note-732][^cite_note-733]&#x20;

### Concentration of executive power

A month after the beginning of the presidency, the government had been sued 200 times for "executive overreach", which the administration lost for the most part.[^cite_note-ARTE-734] On [Twitter](9988187.md), Trump justified his policies by quoting [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon): "He who saves his Country does not violate any Law".[^cite_note-GroundNews2025-735] In reaction, the American left accused him of acting as a dictator.[^cite_note-Reinstein2025-736][^cite_note-Chiacu2025-737][^cite_note-738]&#x20;

The right-wing and left-wing media both claim that their policies allowed a hitherto concentration of executive power in US history. Experts and newspapers feared a constitutional crisis because of frequent [checks and balances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers) breaches.[^cite_note-GroundNews2025-735] The Trump administration often questioned the legitimacy of judges blocking his executive orders.[^cite_note-Reinstein2025-736][^cite_note-Chiacu2025-737][^cite_note-739]&#x20;

According to *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)*, Trump's new governance based itself on unconditional loyalty towards himself. Trump allegedly implemented a climate where any criticism even among the Republicans is sanctioned. Figures like [Jack Posobiec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Posobiec) and [Laura Loomer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Loomer) acted as ideological safeguards by listing potential dissidents among the party.[^cite_note-740]&#x20;

The administrations openly wanted to concentrate political powers within Trump's hands. They justify it with the [unitary executive theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_executive_theory), alleging that by having been elected and with the approval of the Constitution, Trump is warranted in having unchecked and absolute power on the judicial branch, and on politics in general.[^cite_note-ARTE-734] Legal expert Julien Jeanneney argued that the "unconstitutional" policies of Trump are backed by the [Supreme Court](31737.md), of which a third of the judges had been appointed by Trump during his first presidency, hence making Republican judges the majority. According to Jeanneney most of the judges "seem clearly on Trump's side concerning most of his policies, even when they violate the Court's long-lived principles".[^cite_note-741]&#x20;

Numerous experts of fascism and historians ([Jason Stanley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Stanley), [Marci Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marci_Shore), [Timothy Snyder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_Snyder),[^cite_note-742] [Johann Chapoutot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Chapoutot),[^cite_note-743] Paul Lerner, Anne Berg,[^cite_note-Trouillard2025-744] Diana Garvin, Tiffany Florvil, Claudia Koonz,[^cite_note-745] [Asma Mhalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asma_Mhalla),[^cite_note-746] and Ben Worthy[^cite_note-747]) denounced the authoritarianism of the presidency, which they explicitly [linked to fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_and_fascism). However experts [Christopher R. Browning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_R._Browning),[^cite_note-Trouillard2025-744] [Roger Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Griffin),[^cite_note-748] Janos Bourgeois,[^cite_note-749] Lloyd Cox and Brendon O'Connor[^cite_note-Cox2025-750] contested this comparison, although Browning noted "uncanny resemblances" between Trump and Hitler,[^cite_note-Trouillard2025-744] and Cox and O'Connor talked about "proto-fascist phenomenon".[^cite_note-Cox2025-750]&#x20;

### Academic evaluations



The [V-Dem Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-Dem_Institute) said in 2026 regarding Trump's presidency that "the speed with which American democracy is currently dismantled is unprecedented in modern history".

[^cite_note-VDEM_Report_2026-751]

&#x20;The institute noted executive overreach undermining the [rule of law](25166191.md), suppression and intimidation of media and dissenting voices, loss of legislative constraints, and declining [civil rights](37071.md), equality, and [freedom of expression](21401843.md).

[^cite_note-VDEM_Report_2026-751]



Beginning in October 2025, *The New York Times* editorial board published an Autocracy Index showing erosion of US democracy using various benchmarks, offering "a way to understand how much Mr. Trump is eroding American democracy" since his January 2025 inauguration.

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20260513-752]

Professor [Christina Pagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christina_Pagel) mapped the first actions of the Trump administration in a Venn diagram that identifies "five broad domains that correspond to features of proto-authoritarian states". These five domains are: undermining democratic institutions and the rule of law, dismantling federal government; dismantling social protections and rights, enrichment and corruption; suppressing dissent and controlling information; attacking science, environment, health, arts and education, particularly universities; aggressive foreign policy and global destabilization.[^cite_note-kottke-pagel-753] The journalist Martin Sandu and the authoritarian politics researcher Alex Norris described the maximalist interpretation of [executive power](#Federal_government_and_executive_power) in Trump's second term as president, including sweeping [executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_orders_in_the_second_Trump_presidency), the [federal funding freeze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_federal_government_grant_pause), [actions against political opponents and the media](#Actions_against_political_opponents_and_the_media), [pardons of those involved in the January 6th Capitol attack](78937582.md), the actions of [Elon Musk](909036.md)'s [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency), and the like as an attempted [self-coup](1221872.md).[^cite_note-754][^cite_note-755] The political scientist Lee Morgenbesser argued the actions of DOGE are a form of [state capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_capture).[^cite_note-756]&#x20;

## Internal Republican politics

### MAGA fallout over non-release of Jeffrey Epstein files

On July 7, 2025, the FBI issued a two-page statement saying they had concluded that infamous [sex offender](2957039.md) [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md) did not possess a "[client list](80415168.md)", even though Attorney General [Pam Bondi](4550623.md) had hinted in February that such a document was on her desk. Several right-wing media personalities were highly critical of the decision. And per mainstream sources, FBI Deputy Director [Dan Bongino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Bongino) is considering resigning and had been feuding with Bondi. One of the reasons given for the non-release is the privacy of the victims. At a White House press conference on the same day, President Trump asked a reporter, "Are you still talking about Jeffrey Epstein?"[^cite_note-ABC,_Trump_supporters_angry,_July_7,_2025-758]&#x20;

In late-July 24, several Senator Republicans spoke in favor of releasing the information. [Lindsey Graham](363279.md) ([Republican](32070.md)-[South Carolina](27956.md)) said, "The idea that Trump did something nefarious and Biden sat on it for four years, It's hard to believe. Just put it out. Let people look at it." [Josh Hawley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Hawley) (R-[Missouri](19571.md)) said, "Anybody who ever met him is going to be mentioned, so I don't think that's a big deal ... but nor is it a reason to withhold the documents." [Thom Tillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thom_Tillis) (R-[North Carolina](21650.md)) said, "I'm where I've been every time you always ask me this question. Release the damn files."[^cite_note-ABC_News,_Senate_Republicans,_July_24,_2025-759]&#x20;

On September 3, 2025, at an at large outside press conference on the Capitol steps, legislators were joined by nearly a dozen women who state they had been abused by convicted offender Epstein. Representatives [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna), a California Democrat, [Thomas Massie](28005891.md), a Kentucky Republican and [Marjorie Taylor Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Taylor_Greene), a Georgia Republican and close ally of Trump, urged the House of Representatives to vote to release the full files. House Republican leaders offered an alternative vote to continue investigation by the House Oversight Committee.&#x20;

On September 3, President Trump spoke to reporters from the Oval Office, and said that the call for release of files is a "Democrat hoax that never ends".[^cite_note-CBS_News,_Epstein_Survivors-760][^cite_note-CNN,_files_push,_Sept_3,_2025-761] Similarly, [CNN](62028.md) states that Democrats have "sought to weaponize the Epstein issue against Trump and the GOP in recent weeks, trying to force votes that would put vulnerable Republicans in difficult positions. But the tone of Wednesday's press conference was far less partisan."[^cite_note-CNN,_files_push,_Sept_3,_2025-761]&#x20;

On October 7, Attorney General Bondi testified before the [Senate Judiciary Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Judiciary_Committee) and BBC drew five "takeaways" including "3. Bondi keen to focus on crime" and "4. But Democrats focus on Epstein". However, [Senator John Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Neely_Kennedy) ([Republican](32070.md) - [Louisiana](18130.md)) repeated comments from [Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Lutnick) that Epstein was "the greatest blackmailer ever".[^cite_note-BBC,_Five_takeaways,_7_Oct_2025-762]&#x20;

On November 12, 2025, the petition to force a House vote for the release of the Epstein files by the Department of Justice secured the final required signature from Democratic Rep. [Adelita Grijalva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adelita_Grijalva), the latter being sworn in for her position after the end of the October 2025 government shutdown.[^cite_note-763] On the same day, House Democrats released a series of private emails between Epstein, [Ghislaine Maxwell](32018562.md), and journalist [Michael Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Wolff_\(journalist\)). Among them was an email in which Epstein stated that "Trump knew about the girls," though without it being clear that this refers to Epstein's crimes.[^cite_note-764]&#x20;

On November 18, the [Epstein Files Transparency Act](81594904.md) cleared the House and Senate with overwhelming majorities, requiring the release of all materials relating to the Epstein investigation within 30 days.[^cite_note-wwwbbccom2025-765] This would exclude victims' names, as well as material which depicts sexual abuse. The Justice Department may also withhold any materials whose release would "jeapordise an active federal investigation or ongoing prosecution, provided that such withholding is narrowly tailored and temporary". This fact has drawn concern from certain lawmakers, who argue that recently started, ongoing investigations into Bill Clinton and [Larry Summers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_Summers) may delay the release of some materials.[^cite_note-wwwbbccom2025-765] The Act also requires the executive branch to provide an unredacted "list of all government officials and politically exposed persons" who are included in the material. [CNN](62028.md) has expressed scepticism about this aspect of the Act, arguing in an analysis article that "the administration might play games with whatever discretion it has — say, by releasing information that's politically advantageous but withholding other information using the available justifications."[^cite_note-CNN,_Analysis_–_Will_he_actually_release_them_all?,_Nov_19,_2025-766]&#x20;

Trump has reversed course and now states he favors the release of the material.[^cite_note-ABC_News,_House_to_Vote,_Nov_18,_2025-767] He signed the Epstein Files Transparency Act on November 19, and made a lengthy post on Truth Social which included the claim, "Do not forget — The Biden Administration did not turn over a SINGLE file or page related to Democrat Epstein, nor did they ever even speak about him.[^cite_note-768][^cite_note-769]&#x20;

## Public opinion

Main articles: [Opinion polling on the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_second_Trump_presidency) and [United States presidential approval rating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_approval_rating)



Trump's aggregated presidential approval ratings consistently declined over the first months of his second term, with more disapproval ratings than approval ratings just two months after his election and disapproval ratings rising to over 50% after three months.

[^cite_note-NYTimes_CompositePolling_2025-770]



In a larger context, Trump's approval rating ([Gallup polling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_polling)) after the first year of his second term was the lowest of any president since 1977.

[^cite_note-NYTimes_20251226-771]

Donald Trump began his second term with another historically low job approval rating, only improving on his lowest-ever rating (occurring in his first term) by three percentage points.[^cite_note-Brenan2025-772] President Trump began his first term at 45% job approval, and his second with 47%. According to [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.), "Trump remains the only elected president with sub-50% initial approval ratings".[^cite_note-Brenan2025-772] In a [CBS News](359551.md)/[YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll conducted from February 5–7, 2025, Trump reached a career high poll rating of 53%.[^cite_note-773] According to ABC News, Trump's approval rating at the end of his first 100 days in office was 39%, even lower than his already low 42% approval rating at the end of his first 100 days of his first presidency and the worst of any president's approval ratings after their 100 days in 80 years.[^cite_note-774] By July 2025, Gallup found Trump's approval rating reached the lowest for his second term at 37%, largely driven by declining support from independents,[^cite_note-775] and in August, multiple surveys revealed record or near-record low job approval ratings, which Gallup found to be lower than any modern president.[^cite_note-776]&#x20;

As was the case during Trump's first term,[^cite_note-777][^cite_note-778][^cite_note-779] the president's approval ratings have remained remarkably stable.[^cite_note-780][^cite_note-781][^cite_note-782] His ratings are also very polarized, often with more Republicans than Democrats approving of his leadership.&#x20;

In May 2026, a Washington Post–ABC News–Ipsos poll reported declining approval of Trump's leadership, with respondents expressing dissatisfaction over issues including the Iran war and economic policy.[^cite_note-783] Separately, Navigator Research polling indicated that around one in five voters who supported Donald Trump in the 2024 United States presidential election expressed regret over their vote.[^cite_note-784]&#x20;

### Immigration enforcement and protests against ICE

In early June 2025 in response to street [protests against ICE actions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Los_Angeles_protests), President Trump activated and sent 2,100 members of the [California National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_National_Guard) to the Los Angeles area.[^cite_note-BBC_News,_What_the_National_Guard_has_been_seen_doing_in_Los_Angeles_area,_June_10,_2025-785] [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that the protests were the strongest domestic backlash to Trump since he took office in January,[^cite_note-Pitas_06102025-786] and became a focal point in a national debate over immigration, protest, the use of federal force in domestic affairs, the boundaries of presidential power, and freedom of speech and assembly.[^cite_note-Bose_07082025-787]&#x20;

### June 2025 military parade and "No Kings" protests

On June 14, the Trump administration held the [U.S. Army 250th Anniversary Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Army_250th_Anniversary_Parade) in Washington, D.C., to celebrate the 250th anniversary of the U.S. Army.[^cite_note-BBC_News,_Mass_protests_against_Trump_across_US_as_president_holds_military_parade,_June_14,_20252-788] The parade is estimated to have cost somewhere between \$25 and \$45 million.[^cite_note-BBC_News,_Mass_protests_against_Trump_across_US_as_president_holds_military_parade,_June_14,_20252-788] In protest, [50501](79143789.md), [Indivisible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indivisible_movement), and other organizations[^cite_note-789] organized [No Kings protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_No_Kings_protests) in many American cities. Millions attended what was then the largest protest since Trump was re-elected.[^cite_note-BBC_News,_Mass_protests_against_Trump_across_US_as_president_holds_military_parade,_June_14,_20252-788][^cite_note-790]&#x20;

Two follow up No Kings protests (also referred to as **No Kings 2** and **No Kings 3**) took place across the United States and cities abroad in [October 2025](81279597.md) and [March 2026](82478314.md). The October protests took place in some 2,700 locations across the country, and drew an estimated 7 million attendees,[^cite_note-AJE_millions_no_kings-791][^cite_note-792] The March 2026 protests, held in response to the [killings of Alex Pretti](82203651.md) and [Renée Good](82055905.md), as well as the [2026 Minnesota general strike](82198050.md),[^cite_note-793] drew even more participants. The organizers claim the No Kings 3 protests took place in 3,300 locations and drew an estimated eight to nine million protestors,[^cite_note-794] which would make it the [largest single-day protest in American history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protests_and_demonstrations_in_the_United_States_by_size).[^cite_note-795][^cite_note-CBSNews2026-796][^cite_note-797]&#x20;

### May 2026 polling data

According to a national poll conducted by the Marquette Law School between May 20 and 26, 2026, Donald Trump's approval rating fell to 38 percent. The poll shows that voters' satisfaction with how Trump is handling the economy has dropped to 30 percent, while satisfaction with his performance on managing inflation and the cost of living stands at 22 percent. Satisfaction with gasoline prices fell to 19 percent, with 95 percent of respondents believing that gasoline prices have increased. 18 percent say the United States has achieved its goals in the Iran war, and a 28 percent consider the war to have been worth its costs.[^cite_note-798][^cite_note-799]&#x20;

Never in 250 years has America witnessed a sitting president shield himself and his family from tax scrutiny, after leveraging policies that benefit his own businesses and personal portfolios, as Donald J. Trump has done. Why it matters: This isn't a hidden scandal. Trump has done this publicly and proudly. Last year, we called it the "most unprecedented presidency in 250 years." In doing so, he has set a precedent — once so unfathomable as to be laughable — that it's OK for presidents and family members to make billions off deals affected by government decisions, then use the Justice Department to secure lifetime protection from scrutiny of their past tax returns.
